- **Authorized:** the owner, in an interactive session on 2026-11-19, relayed by rigger-ae at [1429#7014074437](https://github.com/williacj/rigger/issues/1429#issuecomment-7014074437).

Part of the **Temporal** track, a post-v0 exploration. **Author:** the PM.

**The track.** v0 chose redo over resume (`D1`, `D10`), one engine per repository (`D11`), the board as the truth (`R-STATE-1`, `R-STATE-4`) and macOS as the only host (`D13`). The owner now wants resume, more than one host, and Windows and Linux hosts, and names Temporal (temporal.io) as the candidate platform. Three spikes, run in parallel, each answer one question by experiment. None records a decision, amends a requirement or changes `ARCHITECTURE.md`; a finding that calls for one is a proposal in its report.

The shape under test: Temporal workflows take the place of the engine's scheduling and card loop, with one workflow per card whose workflow id is the card, a merge-lane workflow per repository, Schedules for clock triggers and signals for escalation answers. Activities wrap what exists today for the forge, git, worktrees, agent dispatch and process containment. Routing is two-stage: a task queue per operating system (`rigger-macos`, `rigger-linux`, `rigger-windows`), then a queue per host that pins a card to the host holding its worktree. The board becomes a one-way mirror written by full-state, idempotent activities.

---

**The question.** On each operating system a Rigger host could run, can an unprivileged supervisor end every process an agent's dispatch started, including descendants that left its session or its parent, and does that still hold after the supervisor itself is killed outright?

**Why a spike.** Rigger's containment today ends every process in a process group it created and every process of its user working in a dispatch's directory (`R-STATE-17`). Linux and Windows each offer a way to hold a whole tree, cgroup v2 and Job Objects. Whether each is reachable unprivileged, from Node, and closes the escapes a process group leaves open is a property of the host. Reasoning does not settle it.

**What exists already, as context that earns no credit.**

- `D13` rule 1 waits on "the WSL2 spike", which `docs/v0-build-plan.md` §5 defines: Engineer workloads under WSL2, Emend's suites, a cold `cargo` build, and one `setsid` `claude -p` kill. No issue for it exists and `docs/spikes/` holds no report of it, read at `6be13718` and by issue search on 2026-11-19. This card does not run §5's steps and does not answer its verdict.
- #832, merged in #835, made the existing suite's containment pass under Ubuntu on WSL2. It measured today's mechanism on Linux, not cgroups.
- #335's report, `docs/spikes/what-leaves-the-process-group.md`, found on macOS that a headless Claude session's commands leave the dispatch's process group, and that a census of processes working in the dispatch's directory found every survivor.
- `D13` rule 1 bars Windows-specific code in v0 before the WSL2 spike reports. The owner ruled that it covers v0's code only, so this card's Windows code, which is throwaway and never merges, is outside it and needs no waiver ([relay](https://github.com/williacj/rigger/issues/1429#issuecomment-7014074437), answer 2).

**The fixture.** One process tree, run on each operating system from a dispatch directory the spike makes. Each member runs at least 300 s unless ended, so a survivor is distinguishable from a member that finished. Every member works in the dispatch directory except M6.

| Member | macOS and Linux | Windows |
|---|---|---|
| M1 | a direct child of the supervised command | the same |
| M2 | a grandchild | the same |
| M3 | a descendant that starts a new session (`setsid`) | a descendant started detached, in a new process group |
| M4 | a double-forked descendant, reparented when its parent exits | a grandchild whose parent exits |
| M5 | a descendant that ignores `SIGTERM` | not applicable: `TerminateProcess` cannot be ignored |
| M6 | a descendant that starts a new session and changes its working directory to `/` | a descendant started detached that changes its working directory to the drive root |

**The endings.**

- **E1, orderly by the host's tree route:** `cgroup.kill` on Linux; ending the Job Object on Windows; killing the process group on macOS.
- **E1c, orderly by today's route:** the supervisor kills the process group, then ends every process of its user working in the dispatch directory, as `R-STATE-17` requires. Run on macOS and on Linux under WSL2.
- **E2, supervisor killed:** the supervisor is killed outright (`kill -9`; `taskkill /F` or `Stop-Process -Force`) while the tree runs, and nothing else acts. On Windows it runs twice: once with the job's kill-on-job-close limit set, and once without it.

Survival is read at least 10 s after an ending: the bound `docs/v0-build-plan.md` §4, M8, test 2 sets for descendants after Rigger is killed.

**Hosts for each operating system.** Linux is Ubuntu on the WSL2 machine. Windows is native on the Windows machine. macOS is the laptop, for the baseline only, which is light. The Mac mini is not used.

**Scope edges.** Moving a member out of its cgroup deliberately, a Job Object breakaway flag, Linux outside WSL2, and containment of a real agent session are outside this card. A host route needing root is measured only if the unprivileged route is absent, and the report labels it as needing root.

**Hosts, and whose custody each is under.**

- **The WSL2 machine and the Windows machine are the primary hosts.** Heavy or long runs go there. The spike reaches each over SSH from the laptop ([relay](https://github.com/williacj/rigger/issues/1429#issuecomment-7014074437), answer 4), at these hosts:
  - the WSL2 machine: `wsl-hostpc` (`user@10.0.0.29`, port 2222, a Windows port proxy into WSL's sshd; until that sshd answers, WSL is reached through the Windows host with `ssh hostpc wsl.exe -e …`);
  - the Windows machine: `hostpc` (`user@10.0.0.29`, port 2223, Windows OpenSSH).
- **The laptop** (macOS) is enrolled under M8's custody rule ([M8-U45](https://github.com/williacj/rigger/issues/1293#issuecomment-6980322109), [M8-U58](https://github.com/williacj/rigger/issues/1293#issuecomment-6988027619)). It takes light macOS runs only, and none of these:
  - reading, writing or running anything in `~/GitHub/emend-engine`, `~/GitHub/emend-engine-worktrees`, `~/GitHub/emend` or `~/rigger-builds/`, on Emend's board (project 13), or in the williacj/emend repository;
  - a LaunchAgent with the `com.williacj.rigger` prefix, or anything in `~/Library/LaunchAgents/com.williacj.rigger*.plist` or `~/Library/Logs/rigger/`;
  - a Rigger run from a checkout, or one against Emend (`R-SAFE-5`);
  - a git GUI, or an IDE that fetches by itself;
  - a long CPU-heavy job during an M8 canary or window period, which rigger-ae announces.
- **The Mac mini** is under the M8 coordinator's custody and is offline. Before any use, the spike tells rigger-ae on this card and waits for rigger-ae to relay the M8 coordinator's OK. Every command there runs through `~/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/coordinator-tools/suite-slot.sh <tag> -- …`. The spike never touches the `judge-*` or `codex-*` clones there.

**Rules on how it runs.**

- **Where:** throwaway work lives in a directory named `<card>-spike-engineer`, where `<card>` is this issue's number: under the gitignored `spikes/` directory on a host with a Rigger checkout, and under a scratch directory of the spike's own making on any other host. It never merges (`AGENTS.md`, "When you write code"). Nothing runs Rigger from a checkout (`R-SAFE-5`).
- **Transcript:** the spike keeps a full transcript of every command it runs, each with its UTC time and host, and discloses every write it did not plan, with its reason (owner ruling [P32](https://github.com/williacj/rigger/issues/642#issuecomment-6089559859)).
- **Stated intent:** before its first install or host-configuration change on any host, the spike posts a comment on this card listing each one it intends, with the host. A configuration change is anything outside its own directories that persists: a package install, a firewall rule, a WSL or systemd setting, a registry key.
- **Evidence:** every file the acceptance cites by path, except where an item says otherwise, is committed in the report's pull request under `docs/spikes/<report>/`, beside the report, so a judge opens it at the judged head. The transcript is among them.
- **Sibling spikes share the WSL2 machine.** The three Temporal-track spikes may run there at once. Before a WSL restart, a `.wslconfig` or `/etc/wsl.conf` change, or any other step that ends processes it did not start, a spike posts the step on all three cards and waits for each sibling spike, or rigger-ae, to answer that none of its recorded processes would be ended. #1429's Temporal server uses the default ports, 7233 and 8233. #1431's uses 7243 for gRPC and 8243 for the UI.
- **Processes:** the spike records the pid of every process it starts, and ends only those, or processes descended from them (`AGENTS.md`, "End only recorded processes").
- **Claude Code:** any live `claude` run uses a `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` the spike made, never the owner's `~/.claude`.
- **M8 and the live board:** the spike writes nothing to the live Rigger board (project 6), to any card in the M8 milestone, or to any branch but this card's.
- **Temporal:** the local development server (`temporal server start-dev`) is the server for this track. Its docs say it is not intended for production use; this spike measures behaviour, not production fitness.

# Temporal-track spike brief

## Approval
The owner approved the work, relayed by rigger-ae:
- the relay: https://github.com/williacj/rigger/issues/1429#issuecomment-7014074437
- the addendum: https://github.com/williacj/rigger/issues/1429#issuecomment-7014098826

## Hosts
The laptop's `~/.ssh/config` has entries for both remote hosts. They authenticate with the agent key `rigger-coordinator`.

- **`ssh hostpc`**: Windows 10.0.26300 at `user@10.0.0.29`, port 2223.
  - The shell is cmd.
  - Node v24.19.0 is installed. The Temporal CLI is not.
- **`ssh wsl-hostpc`**: `user@10.0.0.29`, port 2222. It does not answer yet, because no sshd runs inside WSL; the owner is fixing that.
  - Until then, reach WSL through Windows: `ssh hostpc wsl.exe -e …`.
  - Ubuntu on kernel 6.18 WSL2, IP 192.168.32.12. Node is not installed.
  - cgroup v2 controllers are present: cpuset cpu io memory hugetlb pids rdma.
  - WSL stops when idle.
- **The Mac mini (`rigger-mini`)**: never use it without rigger-ae relaying the M8 coordinator's OK on your card.

## Shared machine
All three Temporal spikes (#1429, #1430, #1431) share this one PC.
- Ports: #1429 uses 7233/8233 and #1431 uses 7243/8243. #1430 starts no Temporal server.
- Before a WSL restart (`wsl --shutdown` or `--terminate`), a `.wslconfig` or `/etc/wsl.conf` change, a reboot, or any step that ends processes you did not start: post the step on all three cards and wait for the sibling spikes or rigger-ae to clear it.

## Laptop custody
These are M8's rules (M8-U45/U58), and an Emend custody window is open now.

Never read, write or run anything in:
- `~/GitHub/emend`, `~/GitHub/emend-engine` or `~/GitHub/emend-engine-worktrees`;
- `~/rigger-builds/`;
- Emend's board (project 13) or the williacj/emend repo;
- `~/Library/LaunchAgents/com.williacj.rigger*` or `~/Library/Logs/rigger/`.

Also:
- Load no LaunchAgent.
- Open no git GUI, IDE or ChatGPT/Codex app.
- Never run Rigger from a checkout, and never against Emend (R-SAFE-5).
- Keep laptop runs light. Heavy and long runs go on the PC.

## Workspace
- Make your own git worktree at `~/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/<card>-spike-engineer`, on branch `rigger-<card>` from `origin/main`. Never write in `~/GitHub/rigger`.
- Throwaway code goes under the gitignored `spikes/<card>-spike-engineer/`. On a remote host, use a directory of your own making with that same name.

## The report
- The report is a docs-only pull request from `rigger-<card>`, with evidence under `docs/spikes/<report>/`, as your card says.
- `npm test` must pass before you commit or push. Never use `--no-verify`.
- Do not review your own work, and do not merge.

## Conduct
- Keep a full transcript: every command, with its UTC time and host.
- Disclose every unplanned write.
- Post the card's stated-intent comment before the first install or configuration change on any host.
- End only processes you started and whose pid you recorded.
- Any live Claude run uses a test-made `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, never `~/.claude`.
- Never write to the live Rigger board (project 6), M8 cards, or #1293.
- If a rule blocks you, or the acceptance is ambiguous, stop and report back. Never edit your card; only its author changes it.

601:| R-STATE-17 | A process works in a directory when its working directory is that directory or lies under it. A process outside every process group Rigger created, and working in no directory Rigger made for a dispatch, is outside this rule. Except as `R-STATE-19` and `R-STATE-28` allow, Rigger ends every process in a process group it created, and every process of its own user working in a dispatch's directory, whenever any of these holds: | the engine | the test suite | |
602-| R-STATE-7 | — the command Rigger ran in that group returns; | the engine | the test suite | |
603-| R-STATE-8 | — that command runs past its time; | the engine | the test suite | |
604-| R-STATE-9 | — Rigger itself exits, unless it is killed outright: ended by a signal that no process can catch or that Rigger cannot safely handle, by an abort, or by a fatal runtime error. | the engine | the test suite | |
605:| R-STATE-19 | Where Rigger cannot end a process `R-STATE-17` or `R-STATE-10` obliges it to end, it records the process by name and command line as not ended, and waits on it no further. That holds only where Rigger may not signal the process, or where the process outlives Rigger's kill for a bound Rigger sets. Except as `R-STATE-26` allows, where Rigger cannot read which processes a group or a dispatch's directory held, it records that group or directory in their place, and waits on it no further. | the engine | the test suite | |
606-| R-STATE-28 | Where Rigger's own exit runs past a fixed time, it ends each remaining group without searching that group's directories, keeps that group's record, and its next start ends what remains in them. | the engine | the test suite | |
607-| R-STATE-10 | If Rigger is itself killed outright, its next start ends what remains of each dispatch whose process group it recorded, except as `R-STATE-11` and `R-STATE-19` allow. | the engine | the test suite | |
608-| R-STATE-11 | A start that cannot read its record of process groups ends nothing, starts no work, and names the record. A start that cannot tell whether a recorded group is still the one Rigger recorded ends nothing in it, starts no work, and names the group. A later start ends such a group only once it confirms the group is still Rigger's. | the engine | the test suite | |
609-| R-STATE-12 | Except as `R-STATE-26` allows, Rigger records every process it had to end by force. Where it cannot read which processes a group held, it records the group it ended in their place. | the engine | the event record, by the kill events it holds | |
610-| R-STATE-13 | Where Rigger ends without having settled which repository it serves, it reports each process it ended by force on standard error instead of recording it, except as `R-STATE-26` allows. It writes nothing under its target. | the engine | standard error, by the forced ends a Rigger that never settled its repository reports there | |
611-| R-STATE-26 | Rigger's record of forced ends holds only what its reads of the process table can show. Before Rigger kills a process group, its reads may list none of the group's processes for as long as it reads while the system still reports the group present. Rigger then cannot read which processes the group held, as `R-STATE-12` and `R-STATE-19` put it. After the kill, a read shows the processes it lists, because a process Rigger killed can still be reported present while it exits. A process that no read of its group or of a dispatch's directory showed, while those reads showed which processes the group or directory held, is outside `R-STATE-12`, `R-STATE-13` and `R-STATE-19`, and Rigger need not record it. | the engine | the test suite | |

README.md
ABOUTME: Spike findings for card #335: what a headless Claude CLI session and a cold cargo workspace
build leave outside their process group on macOS, and which census could find what they leave.

# What leaves the process group, and whether Rigger could find it

## The answer

The cold `cargo build --workspace` of Tauri's workspace left nothing alive once its group was
empty. `ps` observed 1,571 of its processes, and every one stayed in the group. The
working-directory census saw one more, pid 74625, which `ps` never saw, so whether that one stayed
in the group is unknown. It was not alive at the census or after, so it did not outlive the group
("Workload 2").

The headless Claude CLI session did leave processes outside its group. Claude Code runs every Bash
tool call in a process group of its own, so none of an agent's shell commands is in the group
Rigger created.

On a normal exit, the CLI ended its own `run_in_background` task. It did not end a process that a
foreground command started with `&`. That process lived about 108 s past the group in run 2, and
114 s in run 3, until it finished by itself.

When the group was killed while the session ran, which is the path a timeout takes, both survived.
The background task lived 86 s and the `&` loop 119 s past the group, and each ran to its natural
end.

Of the censuses tried, only one found every survivor: listing every process whose working
directory lies under the workload's directory, which for a dispatch is its worktree. The
environment marker is read back through `ps` only for binaries that are not Apple's. The output
socket was already closed. And the parent walk lost the survivors the moment they were reparented.

I recommend that L0's census change: after the group kill, add a working-directory census of the
dispatch's worktree, and kill and record what it finds. The recommendation and what would reverse
it are at the end.

This report hands over evidence. It changes no code, requirement or recorded decision. The
throwaway harness lives in the gitignored spikes directory of the maker's worktree, under c335, and
is not in this pull request. The appendix at the end lists every process each method saw in each
run.

## Host and versions

### 11. 2026-11-20T00:43:25.249Z -> 2026-11-20T00:43:25.565Z  host=laptop  tool=Bash  {"description": "Write POSIX fixture, listing and supervisor"}
mkdir -p ~/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/1430-spike-engineer/spikes/1430-spike-engineer/fixture && cd $_ && cat > fixture.sh <<'EOF'
#!/bin/bash
# ABOUTME: Card #1430 fixture (macOS and Linux): the supervised command; starts members M1-M6, each sleeping 300 s.
# Usage: bash fixture.sh <run-tag>   (cwd = the dispatch directory). Each member's argv[0] is fx-<run>-M<n>.
R="$1"
# M1: direct child of this command. M2: M1's child (a grandchild of this command).
bash -c "(exec -a fx-$R-M2 sleep 300) & exec -a fx-$R-M1 sleep 300" &
# M3: a descendant that starts a new session.
perl -MPOSIX -e 'POSIX::setsid() or die "setsid: $!"; exec {"sleep"} "fx-'"$R"'-M3", "300"' &
# M4: double-forked; its parent exits at once, so it is reparented.
bash -c "(exec -a fx-$R-M4 sleep 300) & exit 0" &
# M5: ignores SIGTERM (SIG_IGN survives exec).
bash -c "trap '' TERM; exec -a fx-$R-M5 sleep 300" &
# M6: a new session that changes its working directory to /.
perl -MPOSIX -e 'POSIX::setsid() or die "setsid: $!"; chdir "/" or die; exec {"sleep"} "fx-'"$R"'-M6", "300"' &
wait
sleep 300
EOF
cat > list.sh <<'EOF'
#!/bin/bash
# ABOUTME: Card #1430 listing (macOS and Linux): every live process of one run with pid, ppid, pgid, sid, cwd and (Linux) cgroup.
R="$1"
echo "listing run=$R at $(date -u +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%S.%3NZ 2>/dev/null || date -u +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ) on $(uname -s)"
pids=$(ps -axo pid=,args= | grep -E "fx-$R-M|fixture.sh $R\$|supervisor.js [a-z0-9]+ $R " | grep -v grep | awk '{print $1}')
printf '%-7s %-7s %-7s %-7s %-5s %-28s %-40s %s\n' PID PPID PGID SID STAT ARGS CWD CGROUP
for p in $pids; do
  line=$(ps -o pid=,ppid=,pgid=,stat= -p "$p") || continue
  set -- $line
  sid=$(python3 -c "import os,sys;print(os.getsid(int(sys.argv[1])))" "$p" 2>/dev/null)
  args=$(ps -o args= -p "$p")
  if [ -e /proc/$p/cwd ]; then cwd=$(readlink /proc/$p/cwd); cg=$(cut -d: -f3 /proc/$p/cgroup);
  else cwd=$(lsof -a -p "$p" -d cwd -Fn 2>/dev/null | sed -n 's/^n//p'); cg=-; fi
  printf '%-7s %-7s %-7s %-7s %-5s %-28s %-40s %s\n' "$1" "$2" "$3" "$sid" "$4" "$args" "$cwd" "$cg"
done
echo "count=$(echo $pids | wc -w | tr -d ' ')"
EOF
cat > supervisor.js <<'EOF'
// ABOUTME: Card #1430 supervisor (macOS and Linux): starts the fixture in its own process group (and, on Linux,
// in a child cgroup of its own delegated cgroup), lists it, then performs ending E1, E1c, or waits to be killed (E2).
// Usage: node supervisor.js <e1|e1c|e2> <run-tag> <dispatch-dir> <fixture-dir>
'use strict';
const { spawn, spawnSync } = require('node:child_process');
const fs = require('node:fs');
const path = require('node:path');
const [mode, run, dispatch, fxdir] = process.argv.slice(2);
const linux = process.platform === 'linux';
const log = (...a) => console.log(new Date().toISOString(), ...a);
const sleep = (ms) => new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, ms));
const list = () => console.log(spawnSync('bash', [path.join(fxdir, 'list.sh'), run], { encoding: 'utf8' }).stdout);

let tree = null;
if (linux && mode !== 'e1c') {
  const own = fs.readFileSync('/proc/self/cgroup', 'utf8').trim().split(':')[2];
  tree = path.join('/sys/fs/cgroup', own, 'tree');
  fs.mkdirSync(tree);
  log('supervisor cgroup', own, 'tree cgroup', tree);
}
log('supervisor pid', process.pid, 'pgid/sid see listing; mode', mode);
const child = spawn('bash', ['-c', `read _; exec bash ${path.join(fxdir, 'fixture.sh')} ${run}`],
  { cwd: dispatch, detached: true, stdio: ['pipe', 'ignore', 'ignore'] });
log('fixture root pid', child.pid, '(its own process group)');
if (tree) { fs.writeFileSync(path.join(tree, 'cgroup.procs'), String(child.pid)); log('moved', child.pid, 'into', tree); }
child.stdin.write('\n');

(async () => {
  await sleep(3000);
  log('BEFORE ENDING'); list();
  if (tree) log('tree cgroup.procs:', fs.readFileSync(path.join(tree, 'cgroup.procs'), 'utf8').trim().split('\n').join(' '));
  if (mode === 'e2') { log('waiting to be killed outright'); await sleep(600000); return; }
  if (mode === 'e1' && tree) {
    const t0 = process.hrtime.bigint();
    fs.writeFileSync(path.join(tree, 'cgroup.kill'), '1');
    let ms = null;
    while (Number(process.hrtime.bigint() - t0) / 1e6 < 10000) {
      if (/populated 0/.test(fs.readFileSync(path.join(tree, 'cgroup.events'), 'utf8'))) { ms = Number(process.hrtime.bigint() - t0) / 1e6; break; }
      await sleep(1);
    }
    log('E1 cgroup.kill written;', ms === null ? 'cgroup STILL populated after 10 s' : `cgroup.events populated 0 after ${ms.toFixed(2)} ms`);
  } else if (mode === 'e1') {
    process.kill(-child.pid, 'SIGKILL'); log('E1 SIGKILL sent to process group', child.pid);
  } else if (mode === 'e1c') {
    // Today's route (R-STATE-17; src/substrate/process.mjs): SIGKILL the group, then SIGKILL every process of
    // this user whose working directory lies under the dispatch directory, found with lsof as runCommand does.
    process.kill(-child.pid, 'SIGKILL'); log('E1c SIGKILL sent to process group', child.pid);
    const real = fs.realpathSync(dispatch);
    const out = spawnSync('lsof', ['-w', '-n', '-P', '-a', '-d', 'cwd', '-u', String(process.getuid()), '-F', 'pun'], { encoding: 'utf8' }).stdout;
    let pid = null; const hits = [];
    for (const l of out.split('\n')) {
      if (l[0] === 'p') pid = Number(l.slice(1));
      if (l[0] === 'n') { const n = l.slice(1); if ((n === real || n.startsWith(real + '/')) && pid !== process.pid) hits.push(pid); }
    }
    for (const p of hits) { try { process.kill(p, 'SIGKILL'); } catch (e) { /* already gone */ } }
    log('E1c census under', real, 'found and SIGKILLed', hits.join(' ') || 'none');
  }
  await sleep(12000);
  log('AFTER ENDING (+12 s)'); list();
  if (tree) log('tree cgroup.procs:', JSON.stringify(fs.readFileSync(path.join(tree, 'cgroup.procs'), 'utf8')));
  process.exit(0);
})();
EOF
bash -n fixture.sh && bash -n list.sh && node --check supervisor.js && echo ok
--- result ---
ok

cd ~/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/1430-spike-engineer/spikes/1430-spike-engineer && cat > mac-run.sh <<'EOF'
#!/bin/bash
# mac-run.sh <mode> <run>: runs one macOS run; for e2, kills the supervisor outright and lists 12 s later.
S=$HOME/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/1430-spike-engineer/spikes/1430-spike-engineer
mode=$1; run=$2; D=$S/mac/d-$run; mkdir -p $D
node $S/fixture/supervisor.js $mode $run $D $S/fixture > $S/mac/$run.log 2>&1 &
sup=$!; echo "supervisor pid $sup (started by mac-run.sh pid $$)"
if [ $mode = e2 ]; then
  until grep -q 'waiting to be killed' $S/mac/$run.log; do sleep 0.2; done
  kill -9 $sup; echo "$(date -u +%FT%TZ) kill -9 $sup"; wait $sup 2>/dev/null
  sleep 12; echo "$(date -u +%FT%TZ) listing +12 s after kill -9"; bash $S/fixture/list.sh $run
else
  wait $sup; echo "supervisor exited $?"
fi
echo ---- supervisor log; cat $S/mac/$run.log
EOF
chmod +x mac-run.sh; ./t.sh laptop './mac-run.sh e1 m1'

### 18. 2026-11-20T00:45:09.444Z -> 2026-11-20T00:45:10.034Z  host=laptop -> hostpc -> WSL  tool=Bash  {"description": "Write WSL runner and copy fixture to WSL"}
cd ~/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/1430-spike-engineer/spikes/1430-spike-engineer && cat > wsl-run.sh <<'EOF'
#!/bin/bash
# wsl-run.sh <mode> <run> [killmode]: inside WSL, starts the supervisor as a transient systemd user service
# (Delegate=yes, KillMode=process unless given), and for e2 kills it outright, lists +12 s, then a fresh writer kills the orphaned tree cgroup.
S=$HOME/1430-spike-engineer; N=$S/node-v24.19.0-linux-x64/bin/node
mode=$1; run=$2; km=${3:-process}; D=$S/d-$run; mkdir -p $D; L=$S/$run.log; : > $L
echo "runner pid $$ in cgroup $(cut -d: -f3 /proc/self/cgroup); id: $(id)"
systemd-run --user --unit=fx1430-$run -p Delegate=yes -p KillMode=$km --working-directory=$S \
  bash -c "exec $N $S/fixture/supervisor.cjs $mode $run $D $S/fixture >> $L 2>&1"
if [ $mode = e2 ]; then
  until grep -q 'waiting to be killed' $L; do sleep 0.2; done
  sup=$(systemctl --user show -p MainPID --value fx1430-$run); cg=$(systemctl --user show -p ControlGroup --value fx1430-$run)
  echo "supervisor MainPID $sup, unit cgroup $cg"
  kill -9 $sup; echo "$(date -u +%FT%T.%3NZ) kill -9 $sup"
  sleep 12; echo "$(date -u +%FT%T.%3NZ) listing +12 s after kill -9"; bash $S/fixture/list.sh $run
  systemctl --user show -p ActiveState -p SubState -p Result fx1430-$run
  T=/sys/fs/cgroup$cg/tree
  if [ -d $T ]; then
    echo "tree cgroup $T procs: $(tr '\n' ' ' < $T/cgroup.procs)"
    bash -c "echo \"fresh writer pid \$\$\"; t0=\$(date +%s%N); echo 1 > $T/cgroup.kill; until grep -q 'populated 0' $T/cgroup.events; do sleep 0.001; done; echo \"fresh writer: cgroup.kill written, populated 0 after \$(( (\$(date +%s%N)-t0)/1000 )) us\""
    sleep 10; echo "$(date -u +%FT%T.%3NZ) listing +10 s after fresh cgroup.kill"; bash $S/fixture/list.sh $run
  else echo "no tree cgroup at $T (systemd removed the unit cgroup)"; fi
else
  until grep -q 'AFTER ENDING' $L && ! systemctl --user is-active -q fx1430-$run; do sleep 0.5; done
fi
echo ---- supervisor log; cat $L
EOF
{ echo 'mkdir -p ~/1430-spike-engineer/fixture'; for f in fixture/fixture.sh fixture/list.sh fixture/supervisor.cjs wsl-run.sh; do echo "base64 -d > ~/1430-spike-engineer/$f <<'B64'"; base64 < $f; echo B64; done; echo 'chmod +x ~/1430-spike-engineer/wsl-run.sh; ls -la ~/1430-spike-engineer ~/1430-spike-engineer/fixture; sha256sum ~/1430-spike-engineer/fixture/*'; } > p4.sh; ./t.sh wsl-via-hostpc 'cat p4.sh | ssh hostpc wsl.exe -e bash -s  # copies fixture/fixture.sh fixture/list.sh fixture/supervisor.cjs wsl-run.sh as base64'; shasum -a 256 fixture/*

### 31. 2026-11-20T00:55:39.246Z -> 2026-11-20T00:55:39.895Z  host=laptop  tool=Bash  {"description": "Write Windows fixture, supervisor, lister and runner"}
mkdir -p ~/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/1430-spike-engineer/spikes/1430-spike-engineer/fixture-win && cd $_ && cat > win32.cjs <<'EOF'
// ABOUTME: Card #1430 Windows helpers: koffi bindings to kernel32/ntdll Job Object and process calls, and the listing.
'use strict';
const koffi = require('koffi');
const { spawnSync } = require('node:child_process');
const k = koffi.load('kernel32.dll');
const nt = koffi.load('ntdll.dll');
const W = {
  CreateJobObjectW: k.func('void* __stdcall CreateJobObjectW(void*, str16)'),
  OpenJobObjectW: k.func('void* __stdcall OpenJobObjectW(uint32, int, str16)'),
  SetInformationJobObject: k.func('int __stdcall SetInformationJobObject(void*, int, void*, uint32)'),
  QueryInformationJobObject: k.func('int __stdcall QueryInformationJobObject(void*, int, void*, uint32, void*)'),
  AssignProcessToJobObject: k.func('int __stdcall AssignProcessToJobObject(void*, void*)'),
  TerminateJobObject: k.func('int __stdcall TerminateJobObject(void*, uint32)'),
  IsProcessInJob: k.func('int __stdcall IsProcessInJob(void*, void*, _Out_ int*)'),
  OpenProcess: k.func('void* __stdcall OpenProcess(uint32, int, uint32)'),
  CloseHandle: k.func('int __stdcall CloseHandle(void*)'),
  GetCurrentProcess: k.func('void* __stdcall GetCurrentProcess()'),
  GetLastError: k.func('uint32 __stdcall GetLastError()'),
  ReadProcessMemory: k.func('int __stdcall ReadProcessMemory(void*, uint64, void*, uint64, void*)'),
  NtQueryInformationProcess: nt.func('int32 __stdcall NtQueryInformationProcess(void*, int, void*, uint32, void*)'),
};
const KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE = 0x2000;
const jobName = (run) => `Local\\fx1430-${run}`;

function limits(job) {
  const b = Buffer.alloc(144);
  if (!W.QueryInformationJobObject(job, 9, b, 144, null)) return `QueryInformationJobObject(9) failed, error ${W.GetLastError()}`;
  const f = b.readUInt32LE(16);
  return `JOBOBJECT_EXTENDED_LIMIT_INFORMATION.BasicLimitInformation.LimitFlags=0x${f.toString(16).padStart(4, '0')} KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE=${(f & KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE) ? 'set' : 'not set'}`;
}
function setKillOnClose(job) {
  const b = Buffer.alloc(144); b.writeUInt32LE(KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE, 16);
  return W.SetInformationJobObject(job, 9, b, 144);
}
function active(job) {
  const b = Buffer.alloc(48);
  if (!W.QueryInformationJobObject(job, 1, b, 48, null)) return null;
  return { total: b.readUInt32LE(36), active: b.readUInt32LE(40), terminated: b.readUInt32LE(44) };
}
function pidsIn(job) {
  const b = Buffer.alloc(8 + 8 * 512);
  if (!W.QueryInformationJobObject(job, 3, b, b.length, null)) return `failed, error ${W.GetLastError()}`;
  const n = b.readUInt32LE(4); const out = [];
  for (let i = 0; i < n; i++) out.push(Number(b.readBigUInt64LE(8 + 8 * i)));
  return out;
}
function inJob(pid, job) {
  const h = W.OpenProcess(0x1000, 0, pid);
  if (!h) return `OpenProcess error ${W.GetLastError()}`;
  const r = [0]; const ok = W.IsProcessInJob(h, job, r); const e = W.GetLastError(); W.CloseHandle(h);
  return ok ? (r[0] ? 'yes' : 'no') : `IsProcessInJob error ${e}`;
}
function cwdOf(pid) {
  const h = W.OpenProcess(0x0410, 0, pid);
  if (!h) return `?(OpenProcess error ${W.GetLastError()})`;
  try {
    const pbi = Buffer.alloc(48);
    if (W.NtQueryInformationProcess(h, 0, pbi, 48, null) !== 0) return '?(NtQueryInformationProcess)';
    const peb = pbi.readBigUInt64LE(8);
    const p = Buffer.alloc(8); if (!W.ReadProcessMemory(h, peb + 0x20n, p, 8n, null)) return '?(read PEB)';
    const params = p.readBigUInt64LE(0);
    const us = Buffer.alloc(16); if (!W.ReadProcessMemory(h, params + 0x38n, us, 16n, null)) return '?(read params)';
    const len = us.readUInt16LE(0); const buf = us.readBigUInt64LE(8);
    const s = Buffer.alloc(len); if (!W.ReadProcessMemory(h, buf, s, BigInt(len), null)) return '?(read string)';
    return s.toString('utf16le');
  } finally { W.CloseHandle(h); }
}
function processes(run) {
  const ps = `Get-CimInstance Win32_Process -Filter "CommandLine like '%fx1430-${run}-%'" | Select-Object ProcessId,ParentProcessId,SessionId,CommandLine | ConvertTo-Json -Compress`;
  const out = spawnSync('powershell.exe', ['-NoProfile', '-NonInteractive', '-Command', ps], { encoding: 'utf8' }).stdout.trim();
  if (!out) return [];
  const j = JSON.parse(out); return (Array.isArray(j) ? j : [j]).filter((p) => !/Get-CimInstance/.test(p.CommandLine));
}
// job: a handle to the spike's job, or null (then only "in any job" is shown).
function list(run, job) {
  const rows = processes(run);
  const lines = [`listing run=${run} at ${new Date().toISOString()} on Windows (Win32_Process + IsProcessInJob + PEB CurrentDirectory)`,
    'PID     PPID    SESS  IN_SPIKE_JOB IN_ANY_JOB CWD | ROLE'];
  for (const r of rows) {
    const role = (r.CommandLine.match(/fx1430-[a-z0-9]+-(\w+)/) || [])[1];
    lines.push(`${String(r.ProcessId).padEnd(7)} ${String(r.ParentProcessId).padEnd(7)} ${String(r.SessionId).padEnd(5)} ${(job ? inJob(r.ProcessId, job) : 'n/a').padEnd(12)} ${inJob(r.ProcessId, null).padEnd(10)} ${cwdOf(r.ProcessId)} | ${role}`);
  }
  lines.push(`count=${rows.length}`);
  return lines.join('\n');
}
module.exports = { W, KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE, jobName, limits, setKillOnClose, active, pidsIn, inJob, cwdOf, list };
EOF

// ABOUTME: Card #1430 Windows fixture member: node member-win.cjs <tag> <role>; sleeps 300 s, starting its own child first for roles M1 and M6.
'use strict';
const { spawn } = require('node:child_process');
const path = require('node:path');
const [tag, role] = process.argv.slice(2);
if (role === 'M1') spawn(process.execPath, [__filename, tag.replace(/-M1$/, '-M2'), 'leaf'], { stdio: 'ignore' });
if (role === 'M6') process.chdir(path.parse(process.cwd()).root);
setTimeout(() => {}, 300000);

cat > fixture-win.cjs <<'EOF'
// ABOUTME: Card #1430 Windows fixture root (the supervised command): node fixture-win.cjs fx1430-<run>-root; waits for a line on stdin, then starts M1-M4 and M6.
'use strict';
const { spawn } = require('node:child_process');
const path = require('node:path');
const run = process.argv[2].replace(/^fx1430-|-root$/g, '');
const member = path.join(__dirname, 'member-win.cjs');
const tag = (m) => `fx1430-${run}-${m}`;
process.stdin.once('data', () => {
  process.stdin.destroy();
  // M1: a direct child; it starts M2, a grandchild.
  spawn(process.execPath, [member, tag('M1'), 'M1'], { stdio: 'ignore' });
  // M3: a descendant started detached, in a new process group (libuv: DETACHED_PROCESS | CREATE_NEW_PROCESS_GROUP).
  spawn(process.execPath, [member, tag('M3'), 'leaf'], { stdio: 'ignore', detached: true }).unref();
  // M4: a grandchild whose parent (cmd.exe running `start /b`) exits at once.
  spawn('cmd.exe', [`/d /c start "" /b "${process.execPath}" "${member}" ${tag('M4')} leaf`], { stdio: 'ignore', windowsVerbatimArguments: true });
  // M6: a descendant started detached that changes its working directory to the drive root.
  spawn(process.execPath, [member, tag('M6'), 'M6'], { stdio: 'ignore', detached: true }).unref();
  setTimeout(() => {}, 300000);
});
EOF
cat > supervisor-win.cjs <<'EOF'
// ABOUTME: Card #1430 Windows supervisor: creates a named Job Object (kill-on-job-close per mode), starts the fixture root
// detached as Rigger does, assigns it to the job before it starts members, lists, then terminates the job (e1) or waits to be killed (e2k, e2n).
// Usage: node supervisor-win.cjs <e1|e2k|e2n> fx1430-<run>-sup <dispatch-dir>
'use strict';
const { spawn } = require('node:child_process');
const path = require('node:path');
const w = require('./win32.cjs');
const [mode, suptag, dispatch] = process.argv.slice(2);
const run = suptag.replace(/^fx1430-|-sup$/g, '');
const log = (...a) => console.log(new Date().toISOString(), ...a);
const sleep = (ms) => new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, ms));
log('supervisor pid', process.pid, 'mode', mode, 'run', run);
const r = [0]; const ok = w.W.IsProcessInJob(w.W.GetCurrentProcess(), null, r);
log(`IsProcessInJob(GetCurrentProcess(), NULL) before creating a job: returned ${ok}, Result=${r[0] ? 'TRUE (already in a job)' : 'FALSE'}`);
const job = w.W.CreateJobObjectW(null, w.jobName(run));
log('CreateJobObjectW name', w.jobName(run), job ? 'ok' : `failed ${w.W.GetLastError()}`);
if (mode !== 'e2n') log('SetInformationJobObject(JobObjectExtendedLimitInformation, KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE):', w.setKillOnClose(job) ? 'ok' : `failed ${w.W.GetLastError()}`);
log('job limits:', w.limits(job));
const child = spawn(process.execPath, [path.join(__dirname, 'fixture-win.cjs'), `fx1430-${run}-root`], { cwd: dispatch, detached: true, stdio: ['pipe', 'ignore', 'ignore'] });
const h = w.W.OpenProcess(0x1fffff, 0, child.pid);
log('fixture root pid', child.pid, 'AssignProcessToJobObject:', w.W.AssignProcessToJobObject(job, h) ? 'ok' : `failed ${w.W.GetLastError()}`);
w.W.CloseHandle(h);
child.stdin.write('\n');
(async () => {
  await sleep(5000);
  log('BEFORE ENDING'); console.log(w.list(run, job));
  log('job process id list (JobObjectBasicProcessIdList):', JSON.stringify(w.pidsIn(job)), 'accounting:', JSON.stringify(w.active(job)));
  if (mode !== 'e1') { log('waiting to be killed outright'); await sleep(600000); return; }
  const t0 = process.hrtime.bigint();
  log('TerminateJobObject:', w.W.TerminateJobObject(job, 1) ? 'ok' : `failed ${w.W.GetLastError()}`);
  let ms = null;
  while (Number(process.hrtime.bigint() - t0) / 1e6 < 10000) { if (w.active(job).active === 0) { ms = Number(process.hrtime.bigint() - t0) / 1e6; break; } await sleep(1); }
  log(ms === null ? 'job still had an active process 10 s after TerminateJobObject' : `job ActiveProcesses reached 0 after ${ms.toFixed(2)} ms`);
  await sleep(12000);
  log('AFTER ENDING (+12 s)'); console.log(w.list(run, job));
  log('job accounting:', JSON.stringify(w.active(job)));
  process.exit(0);
})();
EOF

// ABOUTME: Card #1430 Windows fresh-process listing and reach: node winlist.cjs <run> [terminate]; tries to open the run's named job, lists, and optionally terminates the job.
'use strict';
const w = require('./win32.cjs');
const [run, act] = process.argv.slice(2);
const job = w.W.OpenJobObjectW(0x1f001f, 0, w.jobName(run));
console.log(new Date().toISOString(), `fresh process pid ${process.pid}: OpenJobObjectW(JOB_OBJECT_ALL_ACCESS, FALSE, "${w.jobName(run)}") ->`, job ? 'handle' : `NULL, GetLastError=${w.W.GetLastError()}`);
if (job) console.log('job limits:', w.limits(job), 'pids:', JSON.stringify(w.pidsIn(job)), 'accounting:', JSON.stringify(w.active(job)));
console.log(w.list(run, job));
if (act === 'terminate' && job) console.log(new Date().toISOString(), 'TerminateJobObject:', w.W.TerminateJobObject(job, 1) ? 'ok' : `failed ${w.W.GetLastError()}`);

// ABOUTME: Card #1430 Windows runner: node win-run.cjs <mode> <run>; starts the supervisor detached, and for e2 modes kills it with taskkill /F and lists +12 s later from a fresh process.
'use strict';
const { spawn, spawnSync } = require('node:child_process');
const fs = require('node:fs'); const path = require('node:path');
const [mode, run] = process.argv.slice(2);
const D = path.join(__dirname, `d-${run}`); fs.mkdirSync(D, { recursive: true });
const L = path.join(__dirname, `${run}.log`); const fd = fs.openSync(L, 'w');
const sleep = (ms) => new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, ms));
const sup = spawn(process.execPath, [path.join(__dirname, 'supervisor-win.cjs'), mode, `fx1430-${run}-sup`, D], { detached: true, stdio: ['ignore', fd, fd] });
console.log(new Date().toISOString(), `runner pid ${process.pid}; supervisor pid ${sup.pid}`);
(async () => {
  if (mode === 'e1') { await new Promise((r) => sup.on('exit', r)); }
  else {
    while (!/waiting to be killed/.test(fs.readFileSync(L, 'utf8'))) await sleep(200);
    const k = spawnSync('taskkill', ['/F', '/PID', String(sup.pid)], { encoding: 'utf8' });
    console.log(new Date().toISOString(), `taskkill /F /PID ${sup.pid}:`, (k.stdout + k.stderr).trim());
    await sleep(12000);
    console.log(new Date().toISOString(), 'fresh listing +12 s after the kill:');
    console.log(spawnSync(process.execPath, [path.join(__dirname, 'winlist.cjs'), run, mode === 'e2n' ? 'terminate' : ''], { encoding: 'utf8' }).stdout);
    if (mode === 'e2n') { await sleep(10000); console.log(new Date().toISOString(), 'fresh listing +10 s after the reach attempt:'); console.log(spawnSync(process.execPath, [path.join(__dirname, 'winlist.cjs'), run], { encoding: 'utf8' }).stdout); }
  }
  console.log('---- supervisor log'); console.log(fs.readFileSync(L, 'utf8'));
})();
EOF

### 36. 2026-11-20T00:56:44.442Z -> 2026-11-20T00:57:04.910Z  host=laptop -> hostpc  tool=Bash  {"description": "Annotate job pids and rerun Windows E1"}
cd ~/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/1430-spike-engineer/spikes/1430-spike-engineer/fixture-win && python3 - <<'EOF'
p='win32.cjs'; s=open(p).read()
s=s.replace("""module.exports = {""","""// Image name and parent of each pid, for annotating a job's process id list.
function names(pids) {
  if (!Array.isArray(pids) || !pids.length) return pids;
  const ps = `Get-CimInstance Win32_Process -Filter "${pids.map((p) => `ProcessId=${p}`).join(' OR ')}" | Select-Object ProcessId,ParentProcessId,Name | ConvertTo-Json -Compress`;
  const out = spawnSync('powershell.exe', ['-NoProfile', '-NonInteractive', '-Command', ps], { encoding: 'utf8' }).stdout.trim();
  const j = out ? [].concat(JSON.parse(out)) : [];
  return pids.map((p) => { const r = j.find((x) => x.ProcessId === p); return r ? `${p}:${r.Name}(ppid ${r.ParentProcessId})` : `${p}:gone`; });
}
module.exports = { names,""")
open(p,'w').write(s)
p='supervisor-win.cjs'; s=open(p).read()
s=s.replace("JSON.stringify(w.pidsIn(job)), 'accounting:'","JSON.stringify(w.names(w.pidsIn(job))), 'accounting:'")
open(p,'w').write(s)
p='winlist.cjs'; s=open(p).read()
s=s.replace("'pids:', JSON.stringify(w.pidsIn(job))","'pids:', JSON.stringify(w.names(w.pidsIn(job)))")
open(p,'w').write(s)
EOF
grep -c names supervisor-win.cjs winlist.cjs; for f in *.cjs; do node --check $f; done; cd .. && ./t.sh hostpc 'scp -q fixture-win/*.cjs hostpc:C:/Users/user/1430-spike-engineer/ && ssh hostpc "cd /d C:\Users\user\1430-spike-engineer && node win-run.cjs e1 x2"'

ABOUTME: What a journal entry is, and how the file names in this directory order the entries.

# Journal

An entry records something learned or something that failed, dated, in as few lines as that
takes. It binds nothing — a rule that came out of an entry is written where rules live, and the
entry says what taught us. `AGENTS.md` holds when an entry is committed.

Each entry is its own file here, and `AGENTS.md`, "Version control", holds the name form. Its
parts are the date and time the entry was written, the card that produced it, and a short form
of the entry's heading.

Two properties follow from that form:

- A card owns every name it writes, so two cards working at once never write one file and never
  collide over one.
- The date and time are fixed width, so the names sort into reading order without being opened,
  oldest first ascending and newest first descending, and no line inside a file carries it.

ABOUTME: Journal for #335 (M2-S1): what a headless Claude CLI session and a cold cargo build left
outside their process group on macOS, and which census found it.

# #335 — What leaves the process group

## What ran

A throwaway Node harness, kept in the gitignored `spikes/`, ran eight runs on macOS 27.0 on
2026-09-27. They were four headless `claude -p` sessions, one cold `cargo build --workspace` of
Tauri's workspace at `tauri-v2.12.0`, and three controls. Every run but one was stated on #335
before it was made. The exception is the second control, under "What failed". The findings, the
readings, the recommendation and every observed process are in
[the report](../spikes/what-leaves-the-process-group.md).

## What we learned

- **Every Bash tool call leaves the group.** Claude Code runs each one in a zsh that leads its own
  process group. A group-only census therefore sees none of a dispatched agent's shell commands.
- **Two routes left survivors.** A `nohup … &` child outlived the group by about 108 s and 114 s
  after a normal exit. A group
  kill during the session left both the background task and that child alive, for 86 s and 119 s.
- **Nothing of a cold cargo build outlived its group.** All 1,571 processes `ps` saw stayed in it.
  One more, seen only by the working-directory census, lived too briefly to read its group.
- **The marker test first read "no environment anywhere".** That was because the first processes I
  read were all `/bin` binaries. The pattern only showed once a `node` process was read beside
  them: `ps -E` returns the environment of a non-Apple binary and withholds that of an Apple
  platform binary. Every survivor the CLI left was an Apple binary.
- **Parent tracking at 50 ms missed the `&` survivor in all three runs that had one.** Its parent
  zsh lived for less than one interval, or was sampled only as a zombie, and the survivor was
  reparented and in a foreign group before its first sample. The working-directory census ran in
  two of those runs, and found the survivor both times.

## What failed

- **My pre-run comment for the second control went up after that run.** A tool guard refused my
  first post, and I ran the control without noticing. The addendum on #335 says so.
- **The first Claude run's prompt used `sleep 120 & …`, which Claude Code's Bash tool refuses
  outright.** That run tested only `run_in_background`.
- **The harness first waited for its mid-run readings before taking the census.** It also read
  `lsof` on a socket fd that had closed and been reused. Both were fixed from run 3, and the report
  discards the affected reading.
- **The first appendix left out the one cargo process only the census saw.** Its generator listed
  a census-only pid only when a named one existed too, and cargo had none. The report now lists
  every entry of every census.

data-ref.test.mjs

cd /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-cjwilliams-GitHub-rigger/17bfb854-c7ed-40ce-af2d-5578b0a83820/scratchpad/libuv && sed -n 70,122p process.c; sed -n 1050,1065p process.c
--- result ---
  /* Create a job object and set it up to kill all contained processes when
   * it's closed. Since this handle is made non-inheritable and we're not
   * giving it to anyone, we're the only process holding a reference to it.
   * That means that if this process exits it is closed and all the processes
   * it contains are killed. All processes created with uv_spawn that are not
   * spawned with the UV_PROCESS_DETACHED flag are assigned to this job.
   *
   * We're setting the JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_SILENT_BREAKAWAY_OK flag so only the
   * processes that we explicitly add are affected, and *their* subprocesses
   * are not. This ensures that our child processes are not limited in their
   * ability to use job control on Windows versions that don't deal with
   * nested jobs (prior to Windows 8 / Server 2012). It also lets our child
   * processes created detached processes without explicitly breaking away
   * from job control (which uv_spawn doesn't, either).
   */
  SECURITY_ATTRIBUTES attr;
  JOBOBJECT_EXTENDED_LIMIT_INFORMATION info;

  memset(&attr, 0, sizeof attr);
  attr.bInheritHandle = FALSE;

  memset(&info, 0, sizeof info);
  info.BasicLimitInformation.LimitFlags =
      JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_BREAKAWAY_OK |
      JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_SILENT_BREAKAWAY_OK |
      JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_DIE_ON_UNHANDLED_EXCEPTION |
      JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE;

  uv_global_job_handle_ = CreateJobObjectW(&attr, NULL);
  if (uv_global_job_handle_ == NULL)
    uv_fatal_error(GetLastError(), "CreateJobObjectW");

  if (!SetInformationJobObject(uv_global_job_handle_,
                               JobObjectExtendedLimitInformation,
                               &info,
                               sizeof info))
    uv_fatal_error(GetLastError(), "SetInformationJobObject");


  if (!AssignProcessToJobObject(uv_global_job_handle_, GetCurrentProcess())) {
    /* Make sure this handle is functional. The Windows kernel has a bug that
     * if the first use of AssignProcessToJobObject is for a Windows Store
     * program, subsequent attempts to use the handle with fail with
     * INVALID_PARAMETER (87). This is possibly because all uses of the handle
     * must be for the same Terminal Services session. We can ensure it is tied
     * to our current session now by adding ourself to it. We could remove
     * ourself afterwards, but there doesn't seem to be a reason to.
     */
    DWORD err = GetLastError();
    if (err != ERROR_ACCESS_DENIED)
      uv_fatal_error(err, "AssignProcessToJobObject");
  }
}
  }

  if (options->flags & UV_PROCESS_DETACHED) {
    /* Note that we're not setting the CREATE_BREAKAWAY_FROM_JOB flag. That
     * means that libuv might not let you create a fully daemonized process
     * when run under job control. However the type of job control that libuv
     * itself creates doesn't trickle down to subprocesses so they can still
     * daemonize.
     *
     * A reason to not do this is that CREATE_BREAKAWAY_FROM_JOB makes the
     * CreateProcess call fail if we're under job control that doesn't allow
     * breakaway.
     */
    process_flags |= DETACHED_PROCESS | CREATE_NEW_PROCESS_GROUP;
    process_flags |= CREATE_SUSPENDED;
  }

### 53. 2026-11-20T01:00:51.146Z -> 2026-11-20T01:00:51.353Z  host=laptop  tool=Bash  {"description": "Read L0 containment section in architecture"}

does not let Rigger persist it. A Rigger that stops between the two dispatches loses the count, as
it does between a maker's two attempts. The next invocation's dispatch of that judge is then a
first failure.

L2 decides whether the card is attempted again, and L3 attempts it under the same claim.

L0's workspace adapter runs every git operation it makes on one repository one at a time. It
retries, a bounded number of times, a fetch that another process's fetch beat to the
remote-tracking ref, and records each retry. This is not L3's repo lane, which orders merges. An
agent's own git commands in its workspace are outside the queue.

A process still in the command's group when the direct child exits is terminated by L0, and recorded
by name and command line where a read before the kill found it; it never changes the exit code. A
process that leaves its group is outside this containment. L0 never stops a process group to contain
it. It reads which processes the group holds, kills the group whole, then kills and reads it again until no member is left alive, with three exceptions:

- Where it cannot read the table, it sends the kill on every look until a bound passes, then records the kill of the group and why, and settles without knowing the group is empty.
- Where a member is one L0 may not signal, or outlives the kill for a second, longer bound, L0 records it by name and command line as a process it could not end, and settles.
- Past exit cleanup's budget, L0 kills the group once and settles, as "Supervising `run`" says.

A process the kill ended that no read before it found is recorded as the kill of the
group, as when L0 cannot read the table, wherever a later read finds it. L0 records only what its
reads of the table show, for a group as for a directory. Before the kill, reads of a group that list
none of its processes while signal 0 still reaches it show nothing. So does a census read that
leaves out the leader while signal 0 still reaches the leader's pid. For either, L0 records the kill
of the group, as for a read that fails. After the kill, a read shows what it lists, because a killed
56:| D13 | macOS is v0's only host | Ratified |

### 54. 2026-11-20T01:00:58.188Z -> 2026-11-20T01:00:58.480Z  host=laptop  tool=Bash  {"description": "Read D13 and build plan section 5"}

## D13 — macOS is v0's only host

**Status:** Ratified.

### Rule

1. v0 runs the engine on macOS, and writes no Windows-specific code before the WSL2 spike
   reports. WSL2 is the route it tries first.
2. What Rigger builds is unconstrained by this. A consumer on macOS can build software that runs
   anywhere.

### Deferred, and what returns it

| Deferred | Returns when |
|---|---|
| A native Windows host, with its own containment backend | The WSL2 spike reports that Engineer workloads do not run under WSL2, or a consumer needs a host Rigger cannot reach through WSL2. |

### Notes

One host keeps the containment mechanics to one process model. Process groups, survivor kills and
worktree paths each behave differently on Windows, and every one of them sits in the core, where a
bug costs a stray process or a lost result.

WSL2 is the cheaper route because it reuses the macOS model rather than adding a second one. The
spike exists because that reuse is an assumption, not a finding.

## D14 — Critical is what a maker revision cannot resolve

**Status:** Ratified.

### Rule

## 5. Spike: Engineer workloads under WSL2 (`type:spike`)

Run before any Windows containment code. Docs-only PR under `docs/spikes/`.

1. `wsl --status`, `wsl -l -v`; record distro and version.
2. In the distro, on the Linux filesystem (not `/mnt/c`): rustup, pinned Node, `gh`, Claude CLI,
   authenticated. Record versions.
3. Tauri Linux build libraries (webkit2gtk 4.1, gtk3, libsoup3, librsvg2, ayatana-appindicator);
   clone Emend.
4. Run `cargo test` in `src-tauri/`, `npm test`, `npm run test:harness`, `npm run test:e2e` under
   WSLg or headless. Record pass/fail per suite and cold `cargo build --workspace` time, against a
   Windows native baseline measured in the same run.
5. One real `claude -p` dispatch started with `setsid`; kill the parent; `pgrep` proves nothing
   survives.
6. List every test that passes on Windows native and fails under WSL2.

Verdict: "WSL2 hosts provisioning and Engineer dispatch for all cards except <list>", or "no,
because <suite or tool>".

### 55. 2026-11-20T01:01:18.226Z -> 2026-11-20T01:01:18.570Z  host=laptop  tool=Bash  {"description": "Extract listing blocks from transcript"}

import re,sys,json
s=open('docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/transcript.md').read()
blocks={}
for m in re.finditer(r'(listing run=(\w+) at (\S+) on .*?\ncount=\d+)', s, re.S):
    blk, run, ts = m.group(1), m.group(2), m.group(3)
    blocks.setdefault(run, [])
    if blk not in [b for _,b in blocks[run]]: blocks[run].append((ts, blk))
for r in blocks: blocks[r].sort()
if len(sys.argv)>1:
    for r in sys.argv[1:]:
        for i,(ts,b) in enumerate(blocks[r]): print(r,i,ts,b.count('\n'))
json.dump(blocks, open(sys.argv[0].replace('blocks.py','blocks.json'),'w'))
EOF

### 61. 2026-11-20T01:04:42.782Z -> 2026-11-20T01:04:42.988Z  host=laptop  tool=Write
{
 "file_path": "/Users/cjwilliams/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/1430-spike-engineer/docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment.md.tmpl",
 "content": "ABOUTME: Spike findings for card #1430: whether cgroup v2 under WSL2 and Job Objects on Windows end an\nagent's whole process tree, measured against the macOS baseline and today's route.\n\n# Whole-tree containment on Linux under WSL2 and on Windows\n\n## The answer\n\nOn Linux under WSL2, the answer is yes. An unprivileged supervisor obtained a cgroup it could write\nto from the systemd user manager, with no root. Writing `cgroup.kill` ended all six fixture\nmembers, including the two that left the session and the one that moved to `/`. The cgroup reported\nno live process 1.66 ms after the write, in both runs that measured it. When the supervisor was\nkilled outright, all six members lived on in the orphaned cgroup. A fresh process that wrote\n`cgroup.kill` to that cgroup ended every one of them.\n\nOn native Windows, the answer is also yes, with one condition. A Node supervisor reached Job Objects\nthrough koffi, an FFI package with a prebuilt binary. Every member it started, M1 to M4 and M6, was\nin its job. `TerminateJobObject` ended all of them. With kill-on-job-close set, killing the\nsupervisor with `taskkill /F` ended every member too. Without kill-on-job-close, every member\noutlived the supervisor. A fresh process could not then reach the job, because its name disappeared\nwith the supervisor's last handle. The condition is that this supervisor ran elevated (High\nMandatory Level). A non-elevated supervisor was not measured.\n\nToday's route (`R-STATE-17`) left M6 alive on both macOS and Linux. M6 is the member that starts a\nnew session and changes its working directory to `/`. The group kill and the working-directory\ncensus found every other member. On macOS, the process-group kill alone also left M3 alive, the\nmember that starts a new session. Killing the supervisor outright left every member alive on macOS,\nas `R-STATE-9` and `R-STATE-10` expect.\n\nI recommend a delegated cgroup ended by `cgroup.kill` for Linux under WSL2, and a Job Object with\nkill-on-job-close for native Windows. Both are under \"Recommendations\", with what would reverse\neach. This report hands over evidence. It changes no code, requirement or recorded decision.\n\n## What ran, and where\n\n| Run | Host | Ending | Route the supervisor was started by |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| m1 | laptop (macOS) | E1, process-group kill | `runners/mac-run.sh`, run locally on the laptop |\n| m2 | laptop | E1c, today's route | the same |\n| m3 | laptop | E2, `kill -9` of the supervisor | the same |\n| w1, w7 | WSL2 | E1, `cgroup.kill` | `ssh hostpc wsl.exe -e bash -s` ran `runners/wsl-run.sh` inside WSL. That started the supervisor inside WSL as a transient systemd user service (`systemd-run --user`) |\n| w2 | WSL2 | E1c, today's route | the same |\n| w5 | WSL2 | E2, `kill -9` of the supervisor, then a fresh `cgroup.kill` writer | the same |\n| w3 | WSL2 | E2, a first attempt | the same. Its fresh-writer loop ran on after the cgroup was removed (under \"What failed\"). w5 replaced it |\n| w6 | WSL2 | E2 with systemd's default `KillMode` (supplementary) | the same |\n| x1, x2 | Windows | E1, `TerminateJobObject` | `ssh hostpc` ran `node win-run.cjs` in the SSH session. That started the supervisor detached |\n| x3 | Windows | E2, `taskkill /F`, kill-on-job-close set | the same |\n| x4 | Windows | E2, `taskkill /F`, kill-on-job-close not set, then a fresh process's reach | the same |\n\nWSL was reached only through `ssh hostpc wsl.exe -e …`, because `wsl-hostpc` did not answer. In every\nWSL run, the process that launched the tree, the Node supervisor, lived inside WSL. It was a child\nof the user's systemd manager (pid 339), as each listing's parent pid column shows. It was never a\nWindows process.\n\n`docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/` holds the evidence:\n\n- `transcript.md`, the full transcript.\n- `fixture/`, the fixture source for macOS and Linux.\n- `fixture-win/`, the fixture source for Windows.\n- `runners/`, the scripts that drove the runs.\n\n## Hosts and versions\n\nEach value below is quoted from the command that printed it. Every command is in\n`transcript.md` at the time given.\n\n| Host | Operating system | Kernel | Node |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| Laptop (00:41:03Z) | `sw_vers`: `ProductName: macOS`, `ProductVersion: 27.2.1`, `BuildVersion: 26B131` | `uname -a`: `Darwin Kernel Version 27.2.0 … RELEASE_ARM64_T6041 arm64` | `node --version`: `v26.13.0` |\n| WSL2 machine (00:41:04Z, 00:41:39Z) | `/etc/os-release`: `PRETTY_NAME=\"Ubuntu 26.04.1 LTS\"` | `uname -r`: `6.18.58.1-microsoft-standard-WSL2` | `node --version`: `v24.19.0`, the official linux-x64 tarball unpacked in the spike's own directory |\n| Windows machine (00:41:04Z) | `ver`: `Microsoft Windows [Version 10.0.26300.9974]` | none separate from the OS build | `node --version`: `v24.19.0` |\n\nOther tools the runs used: `lsof` 4.91 on macOS and 4.99.4 on Ubuntu, perl 5.40.1 on Ubuntu, systemd\n259 (259.5-0ubuntu3.4), npm 11.17.0 on Windows, and koffi 3.3.8.\n\n## The fixture\n\nThe fixture is one process tree started in a dispatch directory the spike made: `mac/d-<run>` in\nthe laptop's spike directory, `~/1430-spike-engineer/d-<run>` in WSL, and\n`C:\\Users\\user\\1430-spike-engineer\\d-<run>` on Windows. Each member sleeps 300 s unless ended. On\nmacOS and Linux, `argv[0]` is `fx-<run>-M<n>`. On Windows, the command line carries\n`fx1430-<run>-M<n>`.\n\n### How each member was started\n\n| Member | macOS and Linux (`fixture/fixture.sh`, run by `bash` as the supervised command) | Windows (`fixture-win/fixture-win.cjs`, run by `node` as the supervised command) |\n|---|---|---|\n| M1 | `bash -c \"(exec -a fx-R-M2 sleep 300) & exec -a fx-R-M1 sleep 300\" &`: a direct child of the supervised command | `spawn(node, [member-win.cjs, …-M1, 'M1'])`: a direct child |\n| M2 | the subshell M1 forks before it execs, a grandchild whose parent is M1 | M1 runs `spawn(node, [member-win.cjs, …-M2, 'leaf'])`, a grandchild whose parent is M1 |\n| M3 | `perl -MPOSIX -e 'POSIX::setsid(); exec {\"sleep\"} \"fx-R-M3\", \"300\"' &`: a new session | `spawn(node, […-M3], { detached: true })`. libuv makes this `DETACHED_PROCESS \\| CREATE_NEW_PROCESS_GROUP`, a new process group |\n| M4 | `bash -c \"(exec -a fx-R-M4 sleep 300) & exit 0\" &`: its parent exits at once, so it is reparented | `cmd.exe /d /c start \"\" /b node member-win.cjs …-M4 leaf`. Its parent `cmd.exe` exits at once |\n| M5 | `bash -c \"trap '' TERM; exec -a fx-R-M5 sleep 300\" &`: `SIGTERM` is ignored, and `SIG_IGN` survives `exec` | not applicable: `TerminateProcess` cannot be ignored |\n| M6 | `perl -MPOSIX -e 'POSIX::setsid(); chdir \"/\"; exec {\"sleep\"} \"fx-R-M6\", \"300\"' &`: a new session working in `/` | `spawn(node, […-M6, 'M6'], { detached: true })`. The member runs `process.chdir()` to the drive root, `C:\\` |\n\nThe supervised command waits on stdin for one line before it starts any member. The supervisor moves\nit into the cgroup or job first, and then writes the line. That closes the race between spawning\nand assigning. Every supervisor spawned the supervised command with `detached: true`, as\n`runCommand` in `src/substrate/process.mjs` does, so that command leads a process group of its own.\n\n### The supervisors\n\n- **macOS and Linux:** `fixture/supervisor.cjs <e1|e1c|e2> <run> <dispatch> <fixture-dir>`.\n  - On Linux, it reads its own cgroup from `/proc/self/cgroup` and creates a child cgroup `tree`\n    under it. It writes the supervised command's pid into `tree/cgroup.procs`.\n  - E1 on Linux writes `1` to `tree/cgroup.kill`. It then polls `tree/cgroup.events` every 1 ms\n    until it reads `populated 0`.\n  - E1 on macOS sends `SIGKILL` to the process group.\n  - E1c, on both, sends `SIGKILL` to the process group. It then runs\n    `lsof -w -n -P -a -d cwd -u <uid> -F pun`, the argument list `listing` in\n    `src/substrate/process.mjs` builds, and sends `SIGKILL` to every pid whose working directory\n    lies under the dispatch directory's real path.\n  - It lists 3 s after the start and 12 s after the ending.\n- **Windows:** `fixture-win/supervisor-win.cjs <e1|e2k|e2n> fx1430-<run>-sup <dispatch>`.\n  - It creates a job named `Local\\fx1430-<run>` with `CreateJobObjectW`. For e1 and e2k, it sets\n    `JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE` with `SetInformationJobObject(JobObjectExtendedLimitInformation)`.\n    For e2n it leaves the limit unset.\n  - It assigns the supervised command with `AssignProcessToJobObject`.\n  - E1 calls `TerminateJobObject`. It then polls `JobObjectBasicAccountingInformation.ActiveProcesses`\n    until the count is 0.\n  - It lists 5 s after the start and 12 s after the ending.\n- **The listings:**\n  - `fixture/list.sh` uses `ps` for pid, parent pid, group and state, and `os.getsid` for the\n    session. It takes the working directory from `/proc/<pid>/cwd` on Linux and from `lsof -d cwd`\n    on macOS, and the cgroup from `/proc/<pid>/cgroup` on Linux.\n  - `fixture-win/win32.cjs` uses `Win32_Process` for pid, parent pid and session. It calls\n    `IsProcessInJob` against the spike's job and against any job. It reads the working directory\n    from the process's PEB (`RTL_USER_PROCESS_PARAMETERS.CurrentDirectory`).\n\n## Linux under WSL2\n\n### The user, cgroup v2, systemd and `cgroup.kill`\n\n**The user (w7, 01:01:53Z).** The unit's `ExecStart` ran `id`, and then `exec`ed into the Node\nsupervisor in the same process. It printed:\n\n```\nuid=1000(user) gid=1000(user) groups=1000(user),4(adm),24(cdrom),27(sudo),30(dip),46(plugdev),100(users)\n```\n\nRuns w1 to w6 started the supervisor the same way, without the `id` prefix. Their runner printed the\nsame `id` line. The supervisor ran as the unprivileged user `user`, not root. The user is in `sudo`,\nbut nothing used it.\n\n**cgroup v2 is mounted, and the unified hierarchy is in use (00:41:17Z).** `mount | grep cgroup`\nprinted one line, a `cgroup2` mount at `/sys/fs/cgroup` and no v1 hierarchy:\n\n```\ncgroup2 on /sys/fs/cgroup type cgroup2 (rw,nosuid,nodev,noexec,relatime,nsdelegate)\n```\n\n`cat /sys/fs/cgroup/cgroup.controllers` printed `cpuset cpu io memory hugetlb pids rdma`. At\n00:41:04Z, `stat -fc %T /sys/fs/cgroup` printed `cgroup2fs`.\n\n**systemd runs as pid 1 (00:41:04Z).** `ps -p 1 -o pid,comm` printed:\n\n```\n    PID COMMAND\n      1 systemd\n```\n\nAt 00:41:17Z, `systemctl is-system-running` printed `running`, and `systemctl --user is-system-running`\nalso printed `running`. `loginctl show-user user` showed `Service=user@1000.service` and `State=active`.\n\n**`cgroup.kill` exists (00:41:17Z).** `ls /sys/fs/cgroup` listed `cgroup.kill` at the root.\n`ls -l /sys/fs/cgroup/non-systemd` showed `--w------- 1 root root 0 Nov 19 18:41 cgroup.kill`. The\ndelegated cgroup in the next item holds a `cgroup.kill` owned by `user`.\n\n**An unprivileged user can obtain a writable cgroup subtree, through the systemd user manager\n(00:42:55Z).** Two routes worked, and the runs used the second:\n\n```\n$ systemd-run --user --scope -p Delegate=yes cat /proc/self/cgroup\nRunning as unit: run-p511-i2417.scope; invocation ID: f03feebebecb4105b3116a22f05c16e8\n0::/user.slice/user-1000.slice/user@1000.service/app.slice/run-p511-i2417.scope\n$ systemd-run --user --wait --pipe -q -p Delegate=yes bash -c \"cat /proc/self/cgroup; c=$(cut -d: -f3 /proc/self/cgroup); ls -l /sys/fs/cgroup$c | grep -E \\\"procs|kill|subtree\\\"; id\"\n0::/user.slice/user-1000.slice/user@1000.service/app.slice/run-p512-i2418.service\n--w------- 1 user user 0 Nov 19 18:42 cgroup.kill\n-rw-r--r-- 1 user user 0 Nov 19 18:42 cgroup.procs\n-rw-r--r-- 1 user user 0 Nov 19 18:42 cgroup.subtree_control\nuid=1000(user) gid=1000(user) groups=1000(user),4(adm),24(cdrom),27(sudo),30(dip),46(plugdev),100(users)\n```\n\nThe shell that `wsl.exe -e` starts is not in systemd's tree. Its `/proc/self/cgroup` read\n`0::/non-systemd` at 00:41:04Z, a root-owned cgroup it cannot write to. The scope route moved even\nthat process into a unit of the user manager. The service route starts the process inside the\nunit. Neither needed root, so the root route was not measured.\n\n### Before any ending\n\n@@w1:0@@\n\nThe fixture root, 1844, is the supervised command. Not built |\n| A PowerShell or C# helper process (`Add-Type` P/Invoke) spawned by Node | Nothing: PowerShell ships with Windows | The job handle lives in the helper, not the supervisor, so killing the supervisor tests the helper's lifetime instead. Every call also pays a PowerShell start. Not built |\n\n### Was Node already in a job (00:56:46Z)\n\nBefore it created a job, the supervisor called `IsProcessInJob(GetCurrentProcess(), NULL, &r)`:\n\n```\n2026-11-20T00:56:46.318Z IsProcessInJob(GetCurrentProcess(), NULL) before creating a job: returned 1, Result=TRUE (already in a job)\n```\n\nThe SSH session's processes already sat in a job. Twelve seconds after:\n\n@@m1:1@@\n\nM1, M2, M4 and M5 ended. M3 and M6 were alive. Each leads a session, and so a process group, of its\nown.\n\n### E1c, today's route (m2)\n\n`E1c SIGKILL sent to process group 23291`, then `E1c census under …/mac/d-m2 found and SIGKILLed 23293`.\nTwelve seconds after:\n\n@@m2:1@@\n\nM1 to M5 ended, and M3 ended by the census. Twelve seconds after:\n\n@@m3:1@@\n\nM1 to M6 were all alive, along with the supervised command.\n\n## The table\n\nEach cell gives the state at least 10 s after the ending, and the run whose listing shows it.\nWindows has two E2 rows, because the card runs E2 there twice.\n\n| Member | Ending | macOS (laptop) | Linux (WSL2) | Windows |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| M1 | E1 | ended (m1) | ended (w1, w7) | ended (x2, x1) |\n| M2 | E1 | ended (m1) | ended (w1, w7) | ended (x2, x1) |\n| M3 | E1 | **alive** (m1) | ended (w1, w7) | ended (x2, x1) |\n| M4 | E1 | ended (m1) | ended (w1, w7) | ended (x2, ended (w2) | not run (the card runs E1c on macOS and Linux) |\n| M2 | E1c | ended (m2) | ended (w2) | not run (the same) |\n| M3 | E1c | ended (m2, by the census) | ended (w2, by the census) | not run (the same) |\n| M4 | E1c | ended (m2) | ended (w2) | not run (the same) |\n| M5 | E1c | ended (m2) | ended (w2) | not applicable |\n| M6 | E1c | **alive** (m2) | **alive** (w2) | not run (the same) |\n| M1 | E2 (Windows: kill-on-job-close set) | **alive** (m3) | **alive** (w5) | ended (x3) |\n| M2 | E2 (the same) | **alive** (m3) | **alive** (w5) | ended (x3) |\n| M3 | E2 (the same) | **alive** (m3) | **alive** (w5) | ended (x3) |\n| M4 | E2 (the same) | **alive** (m3) | **alive** (w5) | ended (x3) |\n| M5 | E2 (the same) | **alive** (m3) | **alive** (w5) | not applicable |\n| M6 | E2 (the same) | **alive** (m3) | **alive** (w5) | ended (x3) |\n| M1 | E2, kill-on-job-close not set | not applicable (a Windows job limit) | not applicable (the same) | **alive** (x4) |\n| M2 | E2, the same | not applicable | not applicable | **alive** (x4) |\n| M3 | E2, the same | not applicable | not applicable | **alive** (x4) |\n| M4 | E2, the same | not applicable | not applicable | **alive** (x4) |\n| M5 | E2, the same | not applicable | not applicable | not applicable |\n| M6 | E2, the same | not applicable | not applicable | **alive** (x4) |\n\nAfter E2 on Linux, a fresh `cgroup.kill` writer ended every member (w5). After E2 without\nkill-on-job-close on Windows, a fresh process could not reach the job (x4).\n\n## Recommendations\n\n### Linux under WSL2: a delegated cgroup per dispatch, ended by `cgroup.kill`\n\nThe next card should do these things:\n\n- Obtain a delegated cgroup from the systemd user manager, with `systemd-run --user -p Delegate=yes`\n  or an equivalent D-Bus call.\n- Give each dispatch a child cgroup in it, and place the dispatch's command there before the command\n  starts anything.\n- End the dispatch by writing `cgroup.kill`, and treat the cgroup as ended when `cgroup.events`\n  reads `populated 0` or the cgroup is gone.\n- Record the cgroup's path durably, so that the next start can write `cgroup.kill` to the orphan.\n  That is the cgroup analogue of `R-STATE-10`, which w5's fresh writer showed works.\n\nThis closed every escape the fixture tried, including M6, which today's route left alive (w2). It\nended M5 without a `SIGTERM` stage, and it took 1.66 ms. It needed no root.\n\nThe next card must not assume the following:\n\n- That the systemd user manager exists. Here systemd was pid 1, and `user@1000.service` was running\n  while a login session was open. Neither was tested without a session, nor with WSL's systemd\n  turned off.\n- That systemd's default `KillMode` is harmless. It sends `SIGTERM`, which M5 survived (w6).\n- That a cgroup holds what an agent hands to another manager. A process the agent starts through\n  `systemd-run --user`, or through D-Bus activation, lands in another unit. That was not measured.\n  Moving a member out deliberately is outside this card.\n- That WSL keeps a cgroup across WSL stopping. The brief says WSL stops when idle, and a cgroup does\n  not survive that.\n\n**What would reverse it:**\n\n- A target host without the systemd user manager, or with it but without a delegated subtree.\n- A kernel without `cgroup.kill`.\n- An agent workload shown to escape the dispatch's cgroup through a route Rigger cannot close.\n\nIn the first two cases, the fallback is today's route plus the gap at M6, or a route that needs\nroot.\n\n### Native Windows: a Job Object per dispatch, with kill-on-job-close\n\nThe next card should do these things:\n\n- Create one job per dispatch from the supervisor's own process, through koffi or an equivalent\n  in-process FFI.\n- Set `JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE`, and set no breakaway limit.\n- Assign the dispatch's root before it starts children. Node's `spawn` offers no suspended start,\n  so this needs a gate like the fixture's, or `CreateProcessW` with `CREATE_SUSPENDED` through the\n  FFI.\n- End the dispatch with `TerminateJobObject`.\n\nKill-on-job-close then ends the whole tree when the supervisor is killed outright (x3). That is\nstronger than macOS and Linux, where E2 leaves the tree for the next start.\n\nThe next card must not assume the following:\n\n- That a non-elevated supervisor can do the same. Only an elevated one was measured.\n- That a job without kill-on-job-close can be reached after the supervisor dies. Its name goes with\n  the last handle (x4), so a \"next start ends the remains\" design, as `R-STATE-10` has, does not\n  carry over without a second handle holder. That was not measured.\n- That the outer job a host puts Rigger in is benign. Under SSH, the session's job ended every member\n  on disconnect, which is a containment Rigger did not choose.\n\n**What would reverse it:**\n\n- A non-elevated supervisor that cannot create or assign the job.\n- An agent tool that starts processes with `CREATE_BREAKAWAY_FROM_JOB` under a job that allows it.\n- Resume across a supervisor restart (the Temporal track) turning out to need dispatches that\n  outlive the supervisor, because kill-on-job-close forbids exactly that.\n\n## What this would change, as a proposal\n\nThis section is a proposal for the PM and the architect. The report changes none of these.\n\n| Document | What the recommendation would change |\n|---|---|\n| `D13` | Rule 1 makes macOS v0's only host and bars Windows-specific code before the WSL2 spike reports. A Linux or Windows host with its own containment backend is the deferred row \"A native Windows host, with its own containment backend\" |\n| `R-STATE-17` | Containment is defined as a process group plus a census of the dispatch's directory. A cgroup or a job would be a containment unit that also holds M6, which the definition leaves outside |\n| `R-STATE-9`, `R-STATE-10` and `R-STATE-11` | The killed-outright path: the next start ends each recorded process group, and must confirm that the group is still Rigger's. On Linux the recorded thing would be a cgroup path. On Windows, kill-on-job-close would end the tree at once, and nothing would be left for the next start |\n| `R-STATE-19` and `R-STATE-26` | What Rigger records when it cannot read a group. A cgroup's `cgroup.procs` and a job's process list are different reads |\n| `ARCHITECTURE.md`, \"Failure model\" | L0's containment paragraph: \"A process that leaves its group is outside this containment\" |\n| `src/substrate/process.mjs` (`runCommand`) | The code the paragraph describes. This is a pointer for the architect, not a requirement |\n\n## `docs/v0-build-plan.md` §5\n\nThis spike ran none of §5's steps. It ran no `wsl --status` or `wsl -l -v`, no rustup, `gh`, Claude\nCLI or Tauri libraries, no Emend suite, no `cargo` build, no `claude -p` dispatch, and no Windows\nnative test comparison.\n\nIt did unpack a pinned Node in WSL, which resembles part of step 2, but only as the fixture's\nruntime, in the spike's own directory, and removed afterwards. It answers nothing of §5's verdict.\n\n## What failed\n\n- **The first WSL run lost its arguments.** `ssh hostpc wsl.exe -e bash -lc \"… e1 w1\"` passed\n  through `cmd.exe`, and the runner started with no arguments. It started a supervisor (pid 630)\n  that crashed, and a unit `fx1430-.service` that failed. I then ended the runner (pid 618). From\n  then on, every WSL command went in as a script on stdin.\n- **A direct inspection command broke in `cmd.exe`.** `cmd.exe` split it at its pipes, and it\n  returned `'fx-w1' is not recognized`. It was run outside the transcript wrapper, so the\n  transcript's appendix records it.\n- **w3's fresh-writer loop never ended.** It polled `cgroup.events` until `populated 0`, but systemd\n  removed the cgroup once it emptied. The loop then spun on `grep` errors (20,209 lines) until I\n  ended it (pid 2377). It forked many short processes, which is why WSL's pids jumped from about\n  2,400 to 103,000. Its reading stood, because w3's listing at 00:51:55Z read `count=0`. w5 reran\n  E2 with a loop that also stops when the cgroup is gone.\n- **The first macOS run failed to start.** The worktree's `package.json` sets `\"type\": \"module\"`, so\n  `supervisor.js` failed to load. It was renamed to `supervisor.cjs`.\n- **`list.sh` printed a literal `3N` in its first timestamps**, because macOS `date` has no `%N`. It\n  was fixed after m1. m1's listings carry `.3NZ`.\n- **`win-run.cjs` relayed `$$` into a systemd `ExecStart` for w7.** systemd read it as a literal `$`,\n  so the line `supervisor-process pid $` carries no pid. The `id` line above it is still the\n  supervisor process's own, because `ExecStart` `exec`s into Node. The unit's main pid, 104328, is\n  the supervisor's pid in the listing.\n\n## Custody\n\n### The transcript\n\n`docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/transcript.md` holds every command run through `runners/t.sh`,\neach with its UTC start time and host. Its appendix lists the commands run without the wrapper,\nwith the UTC window each fell in, and two marked edits: a redaction, and the collapsed loop output.\n\n### The credential scan\n\n@@scan@@\n\n### Files written outside the spike's own directories\n\n| Host | File | Reason | Removed |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| WSL2 | `/run/user/1000/systemd/transient/fx1430-*.service`, one per run (`fx1430-`, w1 to w3, w5 to w7), and those for the probe units `run-p511-i2417.scope` and `run-p512-i2418.service` | systemd writes a runtime file for each transient unit `systemd-run --user` starts. ### 65. 2026-11-20T01:05:40.955Z -> 2026-11-20T01:05:41.160Z  host=laptop -> hostpc -> WSL  tool=Bash  {"description": "Write journal entry and doc-references entry"}
