cat > docs/journal/2026-11-20-0105-1430-whole-tree-containment.md <<'EOF'
ABOUTME: Journal for #1430: whether cgroup v2 under WSL2 and Job Objects on Windows end an agent's whole
process tree, against the macOS baseline and today's route.

# #1430 — Whole-tree containment on Linux under WSL2 and on Windows

## What ran

On 2026-11-20 a throwaway fixture ran on three hosts: macOS on the laptop, Ubuntu 26.04 under WSL2,
and native Windows. The fixture was six sleeping members, two of which left the session and one of
which moved to `/`. Each host ran it under each ending the card names. The code stayed in the
gitignored `spikes/`. Its source, every listing and the transcript are in
[the report](../spikes/whole-tree-containment.md).

## What we learned

- **A cgroup cost nothing on this WSL2 host.** systemd was pid 1, and the user manager handed an
  unprivileged user a writable cgroup through `systemd-run --user -p Delegate=yes`. `cgroup.kill`
  ended every member in 1.66 ms. An orphaned cgroup stayed reachable by its path after the
  supervisor died.
- **The shell `wsl.exe -e` starts is outside systemd's tree.** It sits in `/non-systemd`, owned by
  root. A delegated cgroup needs the user manager to start the process, or a scope to move it.
- **A Windows job's name dies with its last handle, not with the job.** Without kill-on-job-close,
  the members outlived the supervisor in a job that no fresh process could open.
  `OpenJobObjectW` failed with error 2.
- **Today's route misses a process that leaves the session and the dispatch directory together.**
  M6 survived the group kill and the census on macOS and on Linux alike.
- **The SSH session is itself a job on Windows,** and it ended every survivor on disconnect.
  Windows readings taken over SSH must finish inside one session.
- **systemd's default `KillMode` sends `SIGTERM`,** which the member that ignores it survived.

## What failed

- **`cmd.exe` ate the arguments of the first WSL run,** and broke one inspection command at its
  pipes. After that, every WSL command went in as a script on stdin.
- **A polling loop waited for `populated 0` from a cgroup that systemd had removed,** and spun until
  I ended it. It forked about 100,000 short processes in WSL. The rerun's loop also stops when the
  cgroup is gone.
- **`supervisor.js` would not load,** because the repository's `package.json` sets
  `"type": "module"`. Throwaway CommonJS in `spikes/` needs the `.cjs` extension.
- **A non-elevated Windows run could not be made.** `runas /trustlevel` failed over SSH, so every
  Windows reading is from an elevated supervisor.
EOF
python3 - <<'EOF'

### 78. 2026-11-20T01:07:01.191Z -> 2026-11-20T01:07:01.302Z  host=laptop  tool=Bash  {"description": "Read path-check rules"}
cd ~/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/1430-spike-engineer && sed -n 1,80p scripts/path-check.mjs
--- result ---
// ABOUTME: Asserts that every backticked repository path in a checked document exists on disk,
// and reads which of the exemptions that excuse one are spent. The resolver never looks at a
// path carrying no line number, so this covers those.

import { existsSync, readFileSync } from 'node:fs';
import { join, resolve, dirname } from 'node:path';
import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';

import { CONFIG, documentChecking } from './doc-reference-check.mjs';

/** A backticked span, which a checked document uses for a path, an id, a column name and a command alike. */
const SPAN = /`([^`\n]+)`/g;
const CODE_MEMBERS = new Set(['String.raw', 'process.exit']);

/**
 * Whether a backticked span names something on disk.
 *
 * A checked document backticks far more than paths, so this asks what a path looks like rather
 * than what exists: a span that exists is a path either way, and a span that does not is the whole
 * point. A path carries no whitespace and names a directory, a dotfile, or a file with a short
 * extension. The checked instructions also name two JavaScript members whose dots name no files.
 */
function looksLikePath(span) {
  if (CODE_MEMBERS.has(span)) return false;
  if (!/^\.?[\w@][\w.@/-]*$/.test(span)) return false;
  return span.includes('/') || /\.[A-Za-z]{1,6}$/.test(span) || /^\.[\w-]+$/.test(span);
}

/** Every backticked repository path in one document, with the line it sits on. */
export function backtickedPaths(text) {
  return text.split('\n').flatMap((line, index) =>
    [...line.matchAll(SPAN)]
      .map((found) => found[1])
      .filter(looksLikePath)
      .map((path) => ({ line: index + 1, path })));
}

/** Whether the prefix of an exemption is still absent, which is what the entry is written for. */
function absent(root, prefix) {
  return !existsSync(join(root, prefix));
}

/**
 * Every exemption prefix the repository now holds, whose entry is therefore spent.
 *
 * The reason beside a prefix is a prose sentence and nothing parses it. What a check can read
 * is the proposition the mechanism acts on: an exemption covers a path while its prefix is
 * absent, so one whose prefix has arrived excuses nothing while a reader goes on believing the
 * sentence. An exemption whose prefix is still absent is correct and reported nowhere, because
 * flagging a correct entry pays the next author to delete it to quieten the build.
 */
export function spentExemptions(root, exemptions) {
  return Object.keys(exemptions).filter((prefix) => !absent(root, prefix)).sort();
}

/**
 * Whether an exemption still covers a path.
 *
 * An exemption is written for a place that does not exist yet, and it lasts exactly that long.
 * Once the place exists, a path under it that is still missing is a broken reference like any
 * other, and the entry in the config is spent.
 */
function exempt(root, path, exemptions) {
  return Object.keys(exemptions).some((prefix) => path.startsWith(prefix) && absent(root, prefix));
}

/**
 * Every backticked path in every checked document that exists nowhere and no exemption covers.
 *
 * The fail level belongs to the resolver, and this check reads past it: a missing path fails the
 * build wherever it sits, a `soft` document included. A pointer at a line is a form to rewrite,
 * and a level says when the rewrite is due. A path nothing answers to is wrong now, so what
 * excuses one here is an exemption naming its reason, never a level.
 */
export function check(root) {
  const { documents, exempt: exemptions } = documentChecking(root);
  const findings = Object.keys(documents).flatMap((document) =>
    backtickedPaths(readFileSync(join(root, document), 'utf8'))
      .filter(({ path }) => !existsSync(join(root, path)) && !exempt(root, path, exemptions.paths))
      .map(({ line, path }) => ({ path: document, line, missing: path })));

### 105. 2026-11-20T01:49:27.866Z -> 2026-11-20T01:49:33.163Z  host=laptop  tool=Bash  {"description": "Push branch and open PR"}
cd ~/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/1430-spike-engineer && git show --stat --format= HEAD | tail -3; git show --name-only --format= HEAD | grep -c "^spikes/"; git push -q -u origin rigger-1430 2>&1 | tail -3; cat > /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-cjwilliams-GitHub-rigger/17bfb854-c7ed-40ce-af2d-5578b0a83820/scratchpad/1430-pr.md <<'EOF'
Closes nothing; spike report for #1430 (card revision `d6218025c943`). Docs-only.

**Findings**
- **Linux under WSL2 (Ubuntu 26.04.1, kernel 6.18.58.1):** an unprivileged user got a delegated, writable cgroup from the systemd user manager (`systemd-run --user -p Delegate=yes`) with no root. `cgroup.kill` ended all of M1 to M6 and reported the cgroup empty in 1.66 ms (two samples). After the supervisor was `kill -9`ed, all six members lived on in the orphaned cgroup, and a fresh `cgroup.kill` writer ended them.
- **Native Windows (10.0.26300):** Node reached Job Objects through koffi (a prebuilt FFI, no compiler). `TerminateJobObject` ended M1 to M4 and M6. With kill-on-job-close set, `taskkill /F` of the supervisor ended them all. Without it, they all survived, and a fresh process could not open the job, because its name went with the last handle (`ERROR_FILE_NOT_FOUND`). The supervisor ran elevated, and no non-elevated run was made.
- **Today's route (`R-STATE-17`)** left M6 (new session, working in `/`) alive on macOS and Linux. On macOS, the group kill alone also left M3.

**Recommendations, as proposals only:** a delegated cgroup per dispatch, ended by `cgroup.kill`, on WSL2. A Job Object with kill-on-job-close on Windows. The report says what would reverse each, and names `D13`, `R-STATE-9/10/11/17/19/26` and `ARCHITECTURE.md` "Failure model" as what they would touch. It changes none of them.

**What this PR holds:** `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment.md`, its evidence (the fixture source, the runners and the full transcript) under `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/`, the journal entry, and the `doc-references.json` entry. Nothing from `spikes/` is in it.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)
EOF

The output says commands “carry no exact time,” labels groups “before 00:41:03,” “after 01:02:17” and “after the pull request opened,” and describes writes as “a python3 edit”/“wrote” rather than preserving their commands. The pre-commit refusal and CI failure are described without their failed outputs. Thus a reader cannot audit every command or the failed runs from the committed artifact. This binds through acceptance item 34, P32's full transcript pattern, AGENTS.md “Documents own their facts,” and the judge brief's rule for missing failed-run output. **B2 — The process census and final cleanup evidence cover a subset (items 41 and 42).** Location: R:748–790; F/fixture/fixture.sh:9–10; T final checks at 00:58:51/00:59:00/01:02:17. Reading-only finding. Exact read-only probe:

The report itself says it lists long-lived processes only, omits synchronous helpers, and never printed the POSIX M4 intermediate PIDs. The last listings query explicit PID lists derived from this subset and searches for fixture member names. A helper or intermediate outside those names is not covered by those queries. I am not claiming one remained alive; the stated all-process proof is missing. This matters because the card expressly requires every process's host/PID and a final listing covering its absence. It binds through items 41/42 and the card's Processes rule. Make it sound with complete recorded host/PID evidence and corresponding final absence evidence, recovering records or repeating affected measurements with complete process recording as needed; disclose historical gaps rather than treating synchronous execution as a substitute for the requested PID inventory.

**Non-blocking observations / carry items**

1. Standard-user Windows containment remains unproved. All Windows supervisors were elevated; the reduced-token command failed. The report discloses this. Not caused by this PR; carry a non-elevated experiment before treating the recommendation as proof of the card's unprivileged-host question.
2. Availability of the systemd user manager without a login session, and orphan-job recovery with a second Windows handle holder, remain unmeasured. Both are disclosed recommendation limits, not introduced regressions. Carry these only when pursuing those deployment/recovery designs.

### 132. 2026-11-20T16:31:57.657Z -> 2026-11-20T16:31:57.890Z  host=laptop  tool=Bash  {"description": "Write rerun recorder, fixture and listing"}
mkdir -p ~/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/1430-spike-engineer/spikes/1430-spike-engineer/r2 && cd $_ && cat > rec.cjs <<'EOF'
// ABOUTME: Card #1430 rerun: records every process this Node process starts (pid, command, times, exit) as JSON lines in $FX_REC.
'use strict';
const cp = require('node:child_process');
const fs = require('node:fs');
const os = require('node:os');
const FILE = process.env.FX_REC;
const HOST = process.env.FX_HOST || os.hostname();
function note(o) {
  const line = JSON.stringify({ t: new Date().toISOString(), host: HOST, by: process.pid, ...o });
  if (FILE) fs.appendFileSync(FILE, line + '\n');
  return line;
}
note({ event: 'self', pid: process.pid, ppid: process.ppid, argv: process.argv });
function spawnSync(cmd, args, opts) {
  const started = new Date().toISOString();
  const r = cp.spawnSync(cmd, args, opts);
  note({ event: 'spawnSync', started, pid: r.pid, cmd, args, status: r.status, signal: r.signal });
  return r;
}
function spawn(cmd, args, opts) {
  const c = cp.spawn(cmd, args, opts);
  note({ event: 'spawn', pid: c.pid, cmd, args });
  c.on('exit', (code, sig) => note({ event: 'exit', pid: c.pid, code, sig }));
  return c;
}
module.exports = { spawnSync, spawn, note };
EOF
cat > fixture.sh <<'EOF'
#!/bin/bash
# ABOUTME: Card #1430 rerun fixture (macOS and Linux): starts M1-M6 sleeping 300 s; every process it starts writes its pid to $FX_SHLOG.
# Usage: bash fixture.sh <run>, cwd = the dispatch directory. Only bash builtins run here between starts, so no unrecorded process is made.
R="$1"; L="$FX_SHLOG"
echo "root $$" >> "$L"
bash -c "echo M1 \$\$ >> '$L'; (exec -a fx-$R-M2 sleep 300) & echo M2 \$! >> '$L'; exec -a fx-$R-M1 sleep 300" &
echo "M1-from-root $!" >> "$L"
perl -MPOSIX -e 'POSIX::setsid() or die "setsid: $!"; exec {"sleep"} "fx-'"$R"'-M3", "300"' &
echo "M3 $!" >> "$L"
bash -c "echo M4-parent \$\$ >> '$L'; (exec -a fx-$R-M4 sleep 300) & echo M4 \$! >> '$L'; exit 0" &
echo "M4-parent-from-root $!" >> "$L"
bash -c "trap '' TERM; echo M5 \$\$ >> '$L'; exec -a fx-$R-M5 sleep 300" &
echo "M5-from-root $!" >> "$L"
perl -MPOSIX -e 'POSIX::setsid() or die "setsid: $!"; chdir "/" or die; exec {"sleep"} "fx-'"$R"'-M6", "300"' &
echo "M6 $!" >> "$L"
wait
exec sleep 300
EOF
cat > list.cjs <<'EOF'
// ABOUTME: Card #1430 rerun listing (macOS and Linux): every live process of one run with pid, ppid, pgid, sid, cwd and (Linux) cgroup,
// read with one ps, plus one python3 (macOS session ids) and one lsof -O (macOS working directories), each recorded by rec.cjs.
'use strict';
const fs = require('node:fs');
const rec = require('./rec.cjs');
const linux = process.platform === 'linux';
const PY = '/Library/Developer/CommandLineTools/Library/Frameworks/Python3.framework/Versions/3.9/bin/python3';
function list(run) {
  const fmt = linux ? 'pid=,ppid=,pgid=,sid=,stat=,args=' : 'pid=,ppid=,pgid=,stat=,args=';
  const out = rec.spawnSync('ps', linux ? ['-eo', fmt] : ['-axo', fmt], { encoding: 'utf8' }).stdout;
  const re = new RegExp(`fx-${run}-M|fixture\\.sh ${run}$|supervisor-posix\\.cjs \\w+ ${run} `);
  const rows = out.split('\n').filter((l) => re.test(l)).map((l) => {
    const f = l.trim().split(/\s+/);
    return linux ? { pid: f[0], ppid: f[1], pgid: f[2], sid: f[3], stat: f[4], args: f.slice(5).join(' ') }
      : { pid: f[0], ppid: f[1], pgid: f[2], stat: f[3], args: f.slice(4).join(' ') };
  });
  if (!linux && rows.length) {
    const s = rec.spawnSync(PY, ['-c', 'import os,sys\nfor p in sys.argv[1:]:\n  try: print(p, os.getsid(int(p)))\n  except Exception as e: print(p, "?")', ...rows.map((r) => r.pid)], { encoding: 'utf8' }).stdout;
    const l = rec.spawnSync('lsof', ['-O', '-w', '-n', '-P', '-a', '-d', 'cwd', '-p', rows.map((r) => r.pid).join(','), '-Fpn'], { encoding: 'utf8' }).stdout;
    const sid = Object.fromEntries(s.trim().split('\n').map((x) => x.split(' ')));
    const cwd = {}; let p = null;
    for (const x of l.split('\n')) { if (x[0] === 'p') p = x.slice(1); if (x[0] === 'n') cwd[p] = x.slice(1); }
    for (const r of rows) { r.sid = sid[r.pid]; r.cwd = cwd[r.pid]; r.cgroup = '-'; }
  }
  if (linux) for (const r of rows) {
    try { r.cwd = fs.readlinkSync(`/proc/${r.pid}/cwd`); } catch { r.cwd = '?'; }
    try { r.cgroup = fs.readFileSync(`/proc/${r.pid}/cgroup`, 'utf8').trim().split(':')[2]; } catch { r.cgroup = '?'; }
  }
  const lines = [`listing run=${run} at ${new Date().toISOString()} on ${linux ? 'Linux' : 'Darwin'}`,
    'PID     PPID    PGID    SID     STAT  CWD | CGROUP | ARGS'];
  for (const r of rows) lines.push(`${r.pid.padEnd(7)} ${r.ppid.padEnd(7)} ${r.pgid.padEnd(7)} ${String(r.sid).padEnd(7)} ${r.stat.padEnd(5)} ${r.cwd} | ${r.cgroup} | ${r.args}`);
  lines.push(`count=${rows.length}`);
  return { text: lines.join('\n'), rows };
}
module.exports = { list };
EOF
echo ok

### 133. 2026-11-20T16:32:13.727Z -> 2026-11-20T16:32:14.081Z  host=laptop  tool=Bash  {"description": "Write rerun POSIX supervisor"}
cd ~/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/1430-spike-engineer/spikes/1430-spike-engineer/r2 && cat > supervisor-posix.cjs <<'EOF'
// ABOUTME: Card #1430 rerun supervisor (macOS and Linux): starts the fixture in its own process group (and, on Linux, in a child
// cgroup of its delegated cgroup), lists it, then performs E1, E1c, or waits to be killed (E2). Every process it starts is recorded.
// Usage: node supervisor-posix.cjs <e1|e1c|e2> <run> <dispatch-dir> <code-dir>
'use strict';
const fs = require('node:fs');
const path = require('node:path');
const rec = require('./rec.cjs');
const { list } = require('./list.cjs');
const [mode, run, dispatch, dir] = process.argv.slice(2);
const linux = process.platform === 'linux';
const log = (...a) => console.log(new Date().toISOString(), ...a);
const sleep = (ms) => new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, ms));
const show = () => console.log(list(run).text);

let tree = null;
if (linux && mode !== 'e1c') {
  const own = fs.readFileSync('/proc/self/cgroup', 'utf8').trim().split(':')[2];
  tree = path.join('/sys/fs/cgroup', own, 'tree');
  fs.mkdirSync(tree);
  log('supervisor cgroup', own, 'tree cgroup', tree);
}
log('supervisor pid', process.pid, 'ppid', process.ppid, 'uid', process.getuid(), 'mode', mode);
const child = rec.spawn('bash', ['-c', `read _; exec bash ${path.join(dir, 'fixture.sh')} ${run}`],
  { cwd: dispatch, detached: true, stdio: ['pipe', 'ignore', 'ignore'] });
log('fixture root pid', child.pid, '(its own process group)');
if (tree) { fs.writeFileSync(path.join(tree, 'cgroup.procs'), String(child.pid)); log('moved', child.pid, 'into', tree); }
child.stdin.write('\n');

(async () => {
  await sleep(3000);
  log('BEFORE ENDING'); show();
  if (tree) log('tree cgroup.procs:', fs.readFileSync(path.join(tree, 'cgroup.procs'), 'utf8').trim().split('\n').join(' '));
  if (mode === 'e2') { log('waiting to be killed outright'); await sleep(600000); return; }
  if (mode === 'e1' && tree) {
    const t0 = process.hrtime.bigint();
    fs.writeFileSync(path.join(tree, 'cgroup.kill'), '1');
    let ms = null;
    while (Number(process.hrtime.bigint() - t0) / 1e6 < 10000) {
      if (/populated 0/.test(fs.readFileSync(path.join(tree, 'cgroup.events'), 'utf8'))) { ms = Number(process.hrtime.bigint() - t0) / 1e6; break; }
      await sleep(1);
    }
    log('E1 cgroup.kill written;', ms === null ? 'cgroup STILL populated after 10 s' : `cgroup.events populated 0 after ${ms.toFixed(2)} ms`);
  } else if (mode === 'e1') {
    process.kill(-child.pid, 'SIGKILL'); log('E1 SIGKILL sent to process group', child.pid);
  } else if (mode === 'e1c') {
    // Today's route (R-STATE-17; src/substrate/process.mjs): SIGKILL the group, then SIGKILL every process of this user
    // working under the dispatch directory, found with lsof. -O added so lsof makes no forked helper process.
    process.kill(-child.pid, 'SIGKILL'); log('E1c SIGKILL sent to process group', child.pid);
    const real = fs.realpathSync(dispatch);
    const out = rec.spawnSync('lsof', ['-O', '-w', '-n', '-P', '-a', '-d', 'cwd', '-u', String(process.getuid()), '-F', 'pun'], { encoding: 'utf8' }).stdout;
    let pid = null; const hits = [];
    for (const l of out.split('\n')) {
      if (l[0] === 'p') pid = Number(l.slice(1));
      if (l[0] === 'n') { const n = l.slice(1); if ((n === real || n.startsWith(real + '/')) && pid !== process.pid) hits.push(pid); }
    }
    for (const p of hits) { try { process.kill(p, 'SIGKILL'); } catch { /* already gone */ } }
    log('E1c census under', real, 'found and SIGKILLed', hits.join(' ') || 'none');
  }
  await sleep(12000);
  log('AFTER ENDING (+12 s)'); show();
  if (tree) log('tree cgroup.procs:', JSON.stringify(fs.readFileSync(path.join(tree, 'cgroup.procs'), 'utf8')));
  process.exit(0);
})();
EOF
node --check supervisor-posix.cjs && echo ok
--- result ---
ok

### 134. 2026-11-20T16:32:42.964Z -> 2026-11-20T16:32:43.260Z  host=laptop  tool=Bash  {"description": "Write rerun POSIX driver"}
cd ~/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/1430-spike-engineer/spikes/1430-spike-engineer/r2 && cat > driver-posix.cjs <<'EOF'
// ABOUTME: Card #1430 rerun driver (macOS and Linux): runs E1, E1c and E2 in turn, starts each supervisor (on Linux as a transient
// systemd user service), kills it for E2, lists, ends survivors, and records every process it starts through rec.cjs.
// Usage: node driver-posix.cjs <run-prefix>   (FX_REC and FX_HOST set by the caller)
'use strict';
const fs = require('node:fs');
const path = require('node:path');
const rec = require('./rec.cjs');
const { list } = require('./list.cjs');
const linux = process.platform === 'linux';
const dir = __dirname;
const prefix = process.argv[2];
const log = (...a) => console.log(new Date().toISOString(), ...a);
const sleep = (ms) => new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, ms));
const alive = (pid) => { try { process.kill(pid, 0); return true; } catch { return false; } };
async function until(test, ms = 120000) { const t = Date.now(); while (!test()) { if (Date.now() - t > ms) throw new Error('timed out'); await sleep(200); } }

async function one(mode, run) {
  const D = path.join(dir, `d-${run}`); fs.mkdirSync(D, { recursive: true });
  const L = path.join(dir, `${run}.log`); fs.writeFileSync(L, '');
  const SH = path.join(dir, `sh-${run}.log`); fs.writeFileSync(SH, '');
  const sup = path.join(dir, 'supervisor-posix.cjs');
  const unit = `fx1430r2-${run}`;
  log(`=== run ${run} mode ${mode}`);
  let exited = null;
  if (linux) {
    const r = rec.spawnSync('systemd-run', ['--user', `--unit=${unit}`, '-p', 'Delegate=yes', '-p', 'KillMode=process',
      `--setenv=FX_REC=${process.env.FX_REC}`, `--setenv=FX_HOST=${process.env.FX_HOST}`, `--setenv=FX_SHLOG=${SH}`, `--working-directory=${dir}`,
      'bash', '-c', `id >> ${L} & echo "id-pid $$!" >> ${SH}; wait $$!; echo "supervisor-bash $$$$" >> ${SH}; exec ${process.execPath} ${sup} ${mode} ${run} ${D} ${dir} >> ${L} 2>&1`], { encoding: 'utf8' });
    log('systemd-run:', (r.stdout + r.stderr).trim());
  } else {
    const fd = fs.openSync(L, 'a');
    const c = rec.spawn(process.execPath, [sup, mode, run, D, dir], { stdio: ['ignore', fd, fd], env: { ...process.env, FX_SHLOG: SH } });
    c.on('exit', () => { exited = true; });
  }
  await until(() => /supervisor pid \d+/.test(fs.readFileSync(L, 'utf8')));
  const supPid = Number(fs.readFileSync(L, 'utf8').match(/supervisor pid (\d+)/)[1]);
  const tree = (fs.readFileSync(L, 'utf8').match(/tree cgroup (\S+)/) || [])[1];
  log('supervisor pid', supPid, tree ? `tree ${tree}` : '');
  if (mode === 'e2') {
    await until(() => /waiting to be killed/.test(fs.readFileSync(L, 'utf8')));
    process.kill(supPid, 'SIGKILL'); log(`kill -9 (process.kill SIGKILL) ${supPid}`);
    await sleep(12000);
    log('listing +12 s after the kill'); console.log(list(run).text);
    if (tree) {
      const code = `const fs=require('fs');const T=process.argv[1];console.log('fresh writer pid',process.pid,'ppid',process.ppid);const t0=process.hrtime.bigint();fs.writeFileSync(T+'/cgroup.kill','1');for(;;){let e;try{e=fs.readFileSync(T+'/cgroup.events','utf8')}catch{e=null}if(e===null||/populated 0/.test(e)){console.log('cgroup.kill written; tree',e===null?'removed':'populated 0','after',(Number(process.hrtime.bigint()-t0)/1e6).toFixed(2),'ms');break}}`;
      const w = rec.spawnSync(process.execPath, ['-e', code, tree], { encoding: 'utf8' });
      log('fresh writer:', (w.stdout + w.stderr).trim());
      await sleep(10000);
      log('listing +10 s after the fresh cgroup.kill'); console.log(list(run).text);
    }
  } else {
    await until(() => /AFTER ENDING/.test(fs.readFileSync(L, 'utf8')) && (linux ? !alive(supPid) : exited));
  }
  const left = list(run).rows;
  for (const r of left) { try { process.kill(Number(r.pid), 'SIGKILL'); log('ended survivor', r.pid, r.args); } catch (e) { log('could not end', r.pid, e.code); } }
  if (linux) { const r = rec.spawnSync('systemctl', ['--user', '--no-pager', 'reset-failed', `${unit}.service`], { encoding: 'utf8' }); log('reset-failed:', r.status, (r.stdout + r.stderr).trim()); }
  await sleep(1000);
  log('after cleanup'); console.log(list(run).text);
  console.log(`---- supervisor log ${run}`); console.log(fs.readFileSync(L, 'utf8'));
  console.log(`---- fixture pid log ${run}`); console.log(fs.readFileSync(SH, 'utf8'));
}
(async () => {
  log('driver pid', process.pid, 'ppid', process.ppid, 'platform', process.platform, 'node', process.version);
  await one('e1', `${prefix}1`);
  await one('e1c', `${prefix}2`);
  await one('e2', `${prefix}3`);
  log('driver done');
})().catch((e) => { console.error(e); process.exit(1); });
EOF

### 135. 2026-11-20T16:32:57.781Z -> 2026-11-20T16:32:58.170Z  host=laptop  tool=Bash  {"description": "Write laptop step runner"}
cd ~/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/1430-spike-engineer/spikes/1430-spike-engineer/r2 && cat > orch.cjs <<'EOF'
// ABOUTME: Card #1430 rerun step runner on the laptop: runs one command (optionally fed a script on stdin), records its pid through
// rec.cjs, and appends the exact argv, stdin script, UTC start and end, host, exit and full output to transcript-r2.txt.
// Usage: FX_REC=... node orch.cjs <host-label> <stdin-file|-> -- <cmd> [args...]
'use strict';
const fs = require('node:fs');
const path = require('node:path');
const crypto = require('node:crypto');
const rec = require('./rec.cjs');
const [host, input, sep, cmd, ...args] = process.argv.slice(2);
if (sep !== '--') throw new Error('usage');
const T = path.join(__dirname, 'transcript-r2.txt');
const start = new Date().toISOString();
const stdin = input === '-' ? null : fs.readFileSync(input);
const c = rec.spawn(cmd, args, { stdio: [stdin ? 'pipe' : 'ignore', 'pipe', 'pipe'], env: process.env });
if (stdin) { c.stdin.end(stdin); }
let out = '';
c.stdout.on('data', (d) => { out += d; process.stdout.write(d); });
c.stderr.on('data', (d) => { out += d; process.stderr.write(d); });
c.on('close', (code, sig) => {
  const end = new Date().toISOString();
  const head = [`### ${start} -> ${end}  host=${host}  orch pid ${process.pid} (ppid ${process.ppid}), child pid ${c.pid}, exit ${code}${sig ? ' signal ' + sig : ''}`,
    '$ ' + JSON.stringify([cmd, ...args])];
  if (stdin) head.push(`stdin: ${input} (sha256 ${crypto.createHash('sha256').update(stdin).digest('hex')}):`, '<<<', stdin.toString().replace(/\n$/, ''), '>>>');
  fs.appendFileSync(T, '\n' + head.join('\n') + '\n--- output ---\n' + out.replace(/\n$/, '') + '\n');
  process.exit(code === null ? 1 : code);
});
EOF
node --check orch.cjs && echo ok
--- result ---
ok

### 139. 2026-11-20T16:34:21.547Z -> 2026-11-20T16:34:21.747Z  host=laptop  tool=Bash  {"description": "Write Windows process watcher"}
cd ~/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/1430-spike-engineer/spikes/1430-spike-engineer/r2 && cat > watcher.ps1 <<'EOF'
# ABOUTME: Card #1430 rerun Windows watcher (fx1430r2): logs every process start and stop on the host, from WMI's temporary
# Win32_ProcessStartTrace and Win32_ProcessStopTrace subscriptions, until C:\Users\user\1430-r2\stop exists.
$dir = 'C:\Users\user\1430-r2'
New-Item -ItemType Directory -Force $dir | Out-Null
$log = "$dir\watch.log"
function W($s) { Add-Content -Path $log -Value $s -Encoding utf8 }
function Now { (Get-Date).ToUniversalTime().ToString('o') }
$p = $PID
for ($i = 0; $i -lt 6 -and $p; $i++) {
  $a = Get-CimInstance Win32_Process -Filter "ProcessId=$p"
  if (-not $a) { break }
  W "chain $(Now) pid=$($a.ProcessId) ppid=$($a.ParentProcessId) name=$($a.Name) created=$($a.CreationDate.ToUniversalTime().ToString('o')) cmd=$($a.CommandLine)"
  $p = $a.ParentProcessId
}
Register-CimIndicationEvent -ClassName Win32_ProcessStartTrace -SourceIdentifier s | Out-Null
Register-CimIndicationEvent -ClassName Win32_ProcessStopTrace -SourceIdentifier e | Out-Null
W "ready $(Now) watcher pid=$PID"
"watcher ready pid=$PID"
while (-not (Test-Path "$dir\stop")) {
  $ev = Wait-Event -Timeout 2
  if ($ev) {
    $n = $ev.SourceEventArgs.NewEvent
    $t = [DateTime]::FromFileTimeUtc([long]$n.TIME_CREATED).ToString('o')
    if ($ev.SourceIdentifier -eq 's') {
      $c = (Get-CimInstance Win32_Process -Filter "ProcessId=$($n.ProcessID)" -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue).CommandLine
      W "start $t pid=$($n.ProcessID) ppid=$($n.ParentProcessID) name=$($n.ProcessName) cmd=$c"
    } else { W "stop $t pid=$($n.ProcessID) ppid=$($n.ParentProcessID) name=$($n.ProcessName)" }
    Remove-Event -EventIdentifier $ev.EventIdentifier
  }
}
Unregister-Event -SourceIdentifier s; Unregister-Event -SourceIdentifier e
W "stopped $(Now)"
"watcher stopped"
EOF
B64=$(iconv -f utf-8 -t utf-16le watcher.ps1 | base64); echo ${#B64}; echo "$B64" > watcher.b64

### 143. 2026-11-20T16:34:55.909Z -> 2026-11-20T16:34:57.046Z  host=laptop  tool=Bash  {"description": "Write Windows rerun driver and bundle files"}
cd ~/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/1430-spike-engineer/spikes/1430-spike-engineer/r2/win && cat > driver-win.cjs <<'EOF'
// ABOUTME: Card #1430 rerun Windows driver: runs E1, E2 with kill-on-job-close and E2 without it, starting each supervisor detached,
// killing it with taskkill /F for E2, listing from fresh processes, ending survivors, and recording every process it starts.
// Usage: node driver-win.cjs   (FX_REC and FX_HOST set by the caller)
'use strict';
const fs = require('node:fs');
const path = require('node:path');
const rec = require('./rec.cjs');
const w = require('./win32.cjs');
const dir = __dirname;
const log = (...a) => console.log(new Date().toISOString(), ...a);
const sleep = (ms) => new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, ms));
const ps = (args) => rec.spawnSync(process.execPath, [path.join(dir, 'winlist.cjs'), ...args], { encoding: 'utf8' }).stdout;
async function one(mode, run) {
  const D = path.join(dir, `d-${run}`); fs.mkdirSync(D, { recursive: true });
  const L = path.join(dir, `${run}.log`); const fd = fs.openSync(L, 'w');
  log(`=== run ${run} mode ${mode}`);
  const sup = rec.spawn(process.execPath, [path.join(dir, 'supervisor-win.cjs'), mode, `fx1430-${run}-sup`, D], { detached: true, stdio: ['ignore', fd, fd] });
  log('supervisor pid', sup.pid);
  if (mode === 'e1') { await new Promise((r) => sup.on('exit', r)); }
  else {
    while (!/waiting to be killed/.test(fs.readFileSync(L, 'utf8'))) await sleep(200);
    const k = rec.spawnSync('taskkill', ['/F', '/PID', String(sup.pid)], { encoding: 'utf8' });
    log(`taskkill /F /PID ${sup.pid}:`, (k.stdout + k.stderr).trim());
    await sleep(12000);
    log('fresh listing +12 s after the kill:'); console.log(ps([run, mode === 'e2n' ? 'terminate' : '']));
    if (mode === 'e2n') { await sleep(10000); log('fresh listing +10 s after the reach attempt:'); console.log(ps([run])); }
  }
  const rows = w.list(run, null).split('\n').filter((l) => /^\d+ /.test(l)).map((l) => Number(l.split(/\s+/)[0]));
  for (const p of rows) { try { process.kill(p, 'SIGKILL'); log('ended survivor', p); } catch (e) { log('could not end', p, e.code); } }
  await sleep(1500);
  log('after cleanup'); console.log(w.list(run, null));
  console.log(`---- supervisor log ${run}`); console.log(fs.readFileSync(L, 'utf8'));
}
(async () => {
  log('driver pid', process.pid, 'ppid', process.ppid, 'node', process.version);
  await one('e1', 'xr1');
  await one('e2k', 'xr2');
  await one('e2n', 'xr3');
  log('driver done');
})().catch((e) => { console.error(e); process.exit(1); });
EOF
for f in *.cjs; do node --check $f || echo BAD $f; done; cd .. && node -e '
const fs=require("fs");const o={};for(const f of fs.readdirSync("win"))o[f]=fs.readFileSync("win/"+f).toString("base64");fs.writeFileSync("win-bundle.json",JSON.stringify(o));console.log(Object.keys(o))'

### 147. 2026-11-20T16:36:40.016Z -> 2026-11-20T16:36:44.913Z  host=laptop -> hostpc -> WSL  tool=Bash  {"description": "Set up WSL rerun directory with recorded processes"}
cd ~/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/1430-spike-engineer/spikes/1430-spike-engineer/r2 && { cat <<'EOF'
# fx1430r2 WSL setup: every external command runs through rec, which prints its pid; only bash builtins run otherwise.
read -r _ _ _ ppid_of_init _ < /proc/$PPID/stat
echo "bash pid $$ ppid $PPID (ppid's own ppid $ppid_of_init) args: $*"
rec(){ "$@" & local p=$!; printf '%(%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ)T rec start pid=%s cmd=%s\n' -1 "$p" "$*"; wait $p; local s=$?; printf '%(%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ)T rec exit pid=%s status=%s\n' -1 "$p" "$s"; return $s; }
D=$HOME/1430-r2
rec mkdir -p $D
cd $D
rec curl -fsSLo node.tar.xz https://nodejs.org/dist/v24.19.0/node-v24.19.0-linux-x64.tar.xz
rec python3 -c 'import tarfile,sys; tarfile.open(sys.argv[1]).extractall(sys.argv[2])' node.tar.xz $D
rec rm node.tar.xz
rec node-v24.19.0-linux-x64/bin/node --version
EOF
for f in rec.cjs list.cjs supervisor-posix.cjs driver-posix.cjs fixture.sh; do echo "rec base64 -d > \$D/$f <<'B64'"; base64 < $f; echo B64; done; echo 'rec sha256sum $D/rec.cjs $D/list.cjs $D/supervisor-posix.cjs $D/driver-posix.cjs $D/fixture.sh'; } > wsl-setup.sh; shasum -a 256 rec.cjs list.cjs supervisor-posix.cjs driver-posix.cjs fixture.sh; export FX_REC=$PWD/rec-laptop.jsonl FX_HOST=laptop && node orch.cjs wsl-via-hostpc wsl-setup.sh -- ssh hostpc wsl.exe -e bash -s fx1430r2

### 151. 2026-11-20T16:37:18.910Z -> 2026-11-20T16:38:18.348Z  host=laptop -> hostpc -> WSL  tool=Bash  {"description": "Run WSL reruns", "timeout": 600000}

### 156. 2026-11-20T16:39:33.699Z -> 2026-11-20T16:39:34.166Z  host=laptop  tool=Bash  {"description": "Derive spike's own Windows processes from watcher log"}
cd ~/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/1430-spike-engineer/spikes/1430-spike-engineer/r2 && cat > collect-win.cjs <<'EOF'
// ABOUTME: Card #1430 rerun: picks out of the Windows watcher log every process this spike started (a process whose command line
// carries the spike's marker, its ancestors below the sshd service, and every descendant of either) and prints them.
// Usage: node collect-win.cjs <watch-log> [<more watch-log>...]  -> JSON on stdout
'use strict';
const fs = require('node:fs');
const MARK = /fx1430|1430-r2/i;
const SERVICE = 4156; // the sshd service process (pid 4156, started 2026-11-10), which belongs to the host
const lines = [...new Set(process.argv.slice(2).flatMap((f) => fs.readFileSync(f, 'utf8').replace(/^﻿/, '').split(/\r?\n/).map((l) => l.replace(/^﻿/, ''))))];
const starts = []; const stops = [];
for (const [i, l] of lines.entries()) {
  let m = l.match(/^(start|chain) (\S+) pid=(\d+) ppid=(\d+) name=(\S+)(?: created=(\S+))? cmd=(.*)$/);
  if (m) { starts.push({ i, kind: m[1], t: m[1] === 'chain' ? m[6] : m[2], pid: +m[3], ppid: +m[4], name: m[5], cmd: m[7] }); continue; }
  m = l.match(/^stop (\S+) pid=(\d+) ppid=(\d+) name=(\S+)/);
  if (m) stops.push({ i, t: m[1], pid: +m[2], name: m[4] });
}
// The watcher's own chain, up to the service, is the spike's: it started that session.
const chain = starts.filter((s) => s.kind === 'chain' && s.pid !== SERVICE && s.ppid !== 1460 && s.pid !== 1584);
for (const s of starts) { const st = stops.find((x) => x.pid === s.pid && x.t >= s.t); s.stop = st ? st.t : null; }
const parentOf = (s) => starts.filter((p) => p.pid === s.ppid && p.t <= s.t && (!p.stop || p.stop >= s.t)).sort((a, b) => (a.t < b.t ? 1 : -1))[0] || null;
const mine = new Set(chain);
for (const s of starts) if (MARK.test(s.cmd)) { mine.add(s); let p = parentOf(s); while (p && p.pid !== SERVICE) { mine.add(p); p = parentOf(p); } }
let grew = true;
while (grew) { grew = false; for (const s of starts) { if (mine.has(s)) continue; const p = parentOf(s); if (p && mine.has(p)) { mine.add(s); grew = true; } } }
// A sibling of a marked process under the same per-connection sshd belongs to that connection too.
grew = true;
while (grew) { grew = false; for (const s of starts) { if (mine.has(s)) continue; const p = parentOf(s); if (p && mine.has(p)) { mine.add(s); grew = true; } } }
const out = [...mine].sort((a, b) => (a.t < b.t ? -1 : 1)).map((s) => ({ pid: s.pid, ppid: s.ppid, name: s.name, started: s.t, stopped: s.stop, cmd: s.cmd.slice(0, 160) }));
console.log(JSON.stringify(out, null, 1));
EOF
node collect-win.cjs watch-1.out > win-mine-1.json; node -e 'const a=require("./win-mine-1.json");console.log(a.length,"mine;", a.filter(x=>!x.stopped).length,"without stop");const c={};for(const x of a)c[x.name]=(c[x.name]||0)+1;console.log(c);console.log(a.filter(x=>!x.stopped).map(x=>x.pid+" "+x.name+" "+x.cmd.slice(0,80)).join("\n"))'

p='collect-win.cjs'; s=open(p).read()
s=s.replace("""// ABOUTME: Card #1430 rerun: picks out of the Windows watcher log every process this spike started (a process whose command line
// carries the spike's marker, its ancestors below the sshd service, and every descendant of either) and prints them.
// Usage: node collect-win.cjs <watch-log> [<more watch-log>...]  -> JSON on stdout""","""// ABOUTME: Card #1430 rerun: picks out of the Windows watcher log every process this spike started and prints them. A process is the
// spike's when it is in the watcher's own session chain, or is a per-connection sshd (a child of the sshd service) that started within
// 3 s of one of the laptop's recorded ssh spawns, or descends from either. A process whose command line carries the marker is checked
// to be among them. Usage: node collect-win.cjs <rec-laptop.jsonl> <watch-log>...  -> JSON on stdout, unmatched connections on stderr""")
s=s.replace("const lines = [...new Set(process.argv.slice(2)","const recLaptop = process.argv[2];\nconst lines = [...new Set(process.argv.slice(3)")
old=s[s.index("const mine = new Set(chain);"):s.index("const out =")]
new='''const sshAt = fs.readFileSync(recLaptop, 'utf8').trim().split('\\n').map((l) => JSON.parse(l))
  .filter((r) => r.event === 'spawn' && r.cmd === 'ssh' && /hostpc/.test(r.args[0] || '')).map((r) => Date.parse(r.t));
const mine = new Set(chain);
for (const s of starts) {
  if (s.ppid !== SERVICE || s.kind !== 'start') continue;
  const near = sshAt.some((t) => Math.abs(Date.parse(s.t) - t) < 3000);
  if (near) mine.add(s); else console.error('unmatched connection, not the spike\\'s:', s.pid, s.t);
}
let grew = true;
while (grew) { grew = false; for (const s of starts) { if (mine.has(s)) continue; const p = parentOf(s); if (p && mine.has(p)) { mine.add(s); grew = true; } } }
for (const s of starts) if (MARK.test(s.cmd) && !mine.has(s)) console.error('marked but not derived:', s.pid, s.t, s.cmd.slice(0, 80));
'''
s=s.replace(old,new)
open(p,'w').write(s)
EOF

### 161. 2026-11-20T16:40:42.702Z -> 2026-11-20T16:40:43.465Z  host=laptop  tool=Bash  {"description": "Collect WSL and laptop pid inventories"}
cd ~/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/1430-spike-engineer/spikes/1430-spike-engineer/r2 && cat > collect-posix.cjs <<'EOF'
// ABOUTME: Card #1430 rerun: gathers every pid a host's records name (rec.cjs JSON lines, fixture pid logs, "bash pid" self-reports and
// "rec start pid=" lines) into one list with where each came from. Usage: node collect-posix.cjs <host> <file>... -> JSON on stdout
'use strict';
const fs = require('node:fs');
const [host, ...files] = process.argv.slice(2);
const seen = new Map();
const add = (pid, what, src) => { pid = Number(pid); if (!pid) return; if (!seen.has(pid)) seen.set(pid, []); seen.get(pid).push(`${what} [${src}]`); };
for (const f of files) {
  for (const l of fs.readFileSync(f, 'utf8').split('\n')) {
    if (l.startsWith('{')) {
      let r; try { r = JSON.parse(l); } catch { continue; }
      if (r.host !== host) continue;
      if (r.event === 'self') { add(r.pid, `self: ${r.argv.slice(1).join(' ').slice(0, 90)}`, f); add(r.ppid, `parent of ${r.pid}`, f); }
      if (r.event === 'spawn' || r.event === 'spawnSync') add(r.pid, `${r.event} by ${r.by}: ${[r.cmd, ...r.args].join(' ').slice(0, 90)}`, f);
      continue;
    }
    let m = l.match(/bash pid (\d+) ppid (\d+) \(ppid's own ppid (\d+)\)/);
    if (m) { add(m[1], 'session bash', f); add(m[2], `WSL init, parent of ${m[1]}`, f); add(m[3], `WSL init, grandparent of ${m[1]}`, f); continue; }
    m = l.match(/rec start pid=(\d+) cmd=(.*)/); if (m) { add(m[1], `rec: ${m[2].slice(0, 90)}`, f); continue; }
    m = l.match(/^(root|M1-from-root|M1|M2|M3|M4-parent-from-root|M4-parent|M4|M5-from-root|M5|M6|id-pid|supervisor-bash) (\d+)$/);
    if (m) add(m[2], `fixture log: ${m[1]}`, f);
  }
}
console.log(JSON.stringify([...seen].map(([pid, why]) => ({ pid, why: [...new Set(why)] })).sort((a, b) => a.pid - b.pid), null, 1));
EOF
sed -n '/=== FILE \/home\/user\/1430-r2\/rec-wsl.jsonl/,/=== FILE \/home\/user\/1430-r2\/wr1.log/p' wsl-fetch.out > rec-wsl.jsonl; sed -n '/=== FILE \/home\/user\/1430-r2\/sh-wr1.log/,$p' wsl-fetch.out > sh-wsl.log
node collect-posix.cjs wsl rec-wsl.jsonl transcript-r2.txt sh-wsl.log wsl-fetch.out > pids-wsl-r2.json; node -e 'const a=require("./pids-wsl-r2.json");console.log(a.length);console.log(a.map(x=>x.pid).join(" "))'
cat sh-mr1.log sh-mr2.log sh-mr3.log > sh-laptop.log; node collect-posix.cjs laptop rec-laptop.jsonl sh-laptop.log > pids-laptop-r2.json; node -e 'const a=require("./pids-laptop-r2.json");console.log(a.length)'

### 164. 2026-11-20T16:41:18.266Z -> 2026-11-20T16:41:22.063Z  host=laptop -> hostpc -> WSL  tool=Bash  {"description": "Final WSL absence check and cleanup"}
cd ~/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/1430-spike-engineer/spikes/1430-spike-engineer/r2 && P=$(node -e 'console.log(require("./pids-wsl-r2.json").map(x=>x.pid).filter(p=>p!==334).join(","))') && cat > wsl-final.sh <<EOF
# fx1430r2 WSL final check: lists any of the spike's recorded WSL pids still alive, shows the boot time, checks no fx1430r2 unit or
# cgroup remains, then removes ~/1430-r2. External commands run through rec, which prints each one's pid.
export TZ=UTC
read -r _ _ _ ppid_of_init _ < /proc/\$PPID/stat
echo "bash pid \$\$ ppid \$PPID (ppid's own ppid \$ppid_of_init) args: \$*"
rec(){ "\$@" <&0 & local p=\$!; printf '%(%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ)T rec start pid=%s cmd=%s\n' -1 "\$p" "\$*" >&2; wait \$p; local s=\$?; printf '%(%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ)T rec exit pid=%s status=%s\n' -1 "\$p" "\$s" >&2; return \$s; }
while read -r k v; do [ "\$k" = btime ] && printf 'boot time (btime) %(%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ)T\n' "\$v"; done < /proc/stat
echo "checking pids: $P"
rec ps -o pid,lstart,args -p $P
echo "ps exit \$? (1 means none of them is alive)"
rec systemctl --user --no-pager list-units --all 'fx1430r2*' --no-legend
rec ls -d /sys/fs/cgroup/user.slice/user-1000.slice/user@1000.service/app.slice/fx1430r2-drv.scope /sys/fs/cgroup/user.slice/user-1000.slice/user@1000.service/app.slice/fx1430r2-wr1.service /sys/fs/cgroup/user.slice/user-1000.slice/user@1000.service/app.slice/fx1430r2-wr2.service /sys/fs/cgroup/user.slice/user-1000.slice/user@1000.service/app.slice/fx1430r2-wr3.service /run/user/1000/systemd/transient/fx1430r2-wr1.service /run/user/1000/systemd/transient/fx1430r2-wr2.service /run/user/1000/systemd/transient/fx1430r2-wr3.service /run/user/1000/systemd/transient/fx1430r2-drv.scope
rec rm -rf \$HOME/1430-r2
rec ls -d \$HOME/1430-r2
EOF
export FX_REC=$PWD/rec-laptop.jsonl FX_HOST=laptop && node orch.cjs wsl-via-hostpc wsl-final.sh -- ssh hostpc wsl.exe -e bash -s fx1430r2 2>&1 | grep -v "^checking"

### 177. 2026-11-20T16:44:18.274Z -> 2026-11-20T16:44:18.477Z  host=laptop -> hostpc -> WSL  tool=Bash  {"description": "Generate full session command log"}
cd ~/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/1430-spike-engineer/spikes/1430-spike-engineer/r2 && cat > gen-session-log.py <<'EOF'
# ABOUTME: Card #1430: turns the spike engineer's retained Claude Code session record (JSON lines) into a plain-text log of every tool
# call, with its UTC issue and completion times, the host it acted on, its exact input and its full result, redacting one account name.
import json, re, sys
src, dst = sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2]
uses, results, order = {}, {}, []
for line in open(src):
    d = json.loads(line)
    if d.get('type') not in ('assistant', 'user') or not d.get('timestamp'):
        continue
    c = d.get('message', {}).get('content')
    if not isinstance(c, list):
        continue
    for x in c:
        if x.get('type') == 'tool_use' and x['id'] not in uses:
            uses[x['id']] = (d['timestamp'], x['name'], x['input']); order.append(x['id'])
        if x.get('type') == 'tool_result':
            body = x.get('content')
            if isinstance(body, list):
                body = '\n'.join(b.get('text', '[non-text block]') if isinstance(b, dict) else str(b) for b in body)
            results[x['tool_use_id']] = (d['timestamp'], bool(x.get('is_error')), body if body is not None else '')
def host(name, inp):
    if name != 'Bash':
        return 'laptop'
    cmd = inp.get('command', '')
    if 'wsl.exe' in cmd or 'wsl-hostpc' in cmd: return 'laptop -> hostpc -> WSL'
    if 'hostpc' in cmd: return 'laptop -> hostpc'
    return 'laptop'
def redact(s):
    s = re.sub(r'MicrosoftAccount\\\S+(\s+)User(\s+)S-1-11-96-[0-9-]+', lambda m: 'MicrosoftAccount\\[redacted: account name]' + m.group(1) + 'User' + m.group(2) + 'S-1-11-96-[redacted]', s)
    s = re.sub(r'[A-Za-z0-9._%+-]+@[name withheld]\.com', '[redacted: account name]', s)
    return s
out = ['ABOUTME: Every tool call the #1430 spike engineer made, generated from its retained Claude Code session record, with UTC times, host, exact input and full result.', '',
       'Generated by gen-session-log.py from the session record of subagent a9a3ffa429348b341. Times are UTC: the first is when the call was issued, the second when its result returned.',
       '"host" is read from the command text: laptop unless the command reaches hostpc over ssh, and WSL when it runs wsl.exe. A command that ran in the background shows its launch result here; its output file is named in the result.',
       'Redactions: one Microsoft account name and its SID, wherever whoami /groups output appears. Nothing else is removed. A call that returned an error is marked ERROR.', '']
for n, i in enumerate(order, 1):
    t0, name, inp = uses[i]
    t1, err, body = results.get(i, (None, False, '[no result recorded: the session record ends before it]'))
    shown = inp.get('command') if name == 'Bash' and set(inp) <= {'command', 'description', 'timeout', 'run_in_background', 'dangerouslyDisableSandbox'} else json.dumps(inp, ensure_ascii=False, indent=1)
    extra = '' if name != 'Bash' else '  ' + json.dumps({k: v for k, v in inp.items() if k != 'command'}, ensure_ascii=False)
    out.append(f'### {n}. {t0} -> {t1}  host={host(name, inp)}  tool={name}{"  ERROR" if err else ""}{extra}')
    out.append(redact(shown if isinstance(shown, str) else str(shown)))
    out.append('--- result ---')
    out.append(redact(str(body)))
    out.append('')
open(dst, 'w').write('\n'.join(out))
print(len(order), 'calls')
EOF
python3 gen-session-log.py ~/.claude/projects/-Users-cjwilliams-GitHub-rigger/17bfb854-c7ed-40ce-af2d-5578b0a83820/subagents/agent-a9a3ffa429348b341.jsonl session-log.txt && wc -c session-log.txt && grep -c "[name withheld]\|S-1-11-96-3" session-log.txt; grep -c "  ERROR" session-log.txt
--- result ---
176 calls
  455804 session-log.txt

### 181. 2026-11-20T16:44:40.409Z -> 2026-11-20T16:44:40.526Z  host=laptop  tool=Bash  {"description": "Read header check rules"}
cd ~/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/1430-spike-engineer && sed -n 1,60p scripts/aboutme-header-check.mjs | grep -v "^ \*\|^/\*\*\|^ \*/" | head -50
--- result ---
// ABOUTME: Refuses a tracked file whose opening header repeats the prefix the rule allows once,
// naming the file and the lines that carry it.

import { readFileSync } from 'node:fs';
import { join, resolve, dirname } from 'node:path';
import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';

import { trackedFiles } from './ruled-out-word-check.mjs';

const RULE_SOURCE = 'AGENTS.md';

export const PREFIX = 'ABOUTME:';

export function headerRun(text) {
  const run = [];
  for (const [index, line] of text.split(/\r?\n/).entries()) {
    if (line.includes(PREFIX)) run.push(index + 1);
    else if (run.length > 0) break;
  }
  return run.length > 0 ? run : null;
}

export function headerRule(agents) {
  const lines = agents.split(/\r?\n/);
  const opens = lines.findIndex((line) => /^-\s+\*\*Every file opens with an/.test(line));
  if (opens < 0) throw new Error('AGENTS.md states no header rule to read the exempt set from');
  const bullet = [lines[opens]];
  for (const line of lines.slice(opens + 1)) {
    if (!/^\s{2,}\S/.test(line)) break;
    bullet.push(line);
  }
  return bullet.join(' ').replace(/\s+/g, ' ').trim();
}

```
## The answer

On Linux under WSL2, the answer is yes. An unprivileged supervisor obtained a cgroup it could write
to from the systemd user manager, with no root. Writing cgroup.kill ended all six fixture
members, including the two that left the session and the one that moved to `/`. The cgroup reported
no live process 1.66 ms after the write, in both runs that measured it. When the supervisor was
killed outright, all six members lived on in the orphaned cgroup. A fresh process that wrote
cgroup.kill to that cgroup ended every one of them.

On native Windows, the answer is also yes, with one condition. A Node supervisor reached Job Objects
through koffi, an FFI package with a prebuilt binary. Every member it started, M1 to M4 and M6, was
in its job. `TerminateJobObject` ended all of them. With kill-on-job-close set, killing the
supervisor with `taskkill /F` ended every member too. Without kill-on-job-close, every member
outlived the supervisor. A fresh process could not then reach the job, because its name disappeared
with the supervisor's last handle. The condition is that this supervisor ran elevated (High
Mandatory Level). A non-elevated supervisor was not measured.

Today's route (`R-STATE-17`) left M6 alive on both macOS and Linux. M6 is the member that starts a
new session and changes its working directory to `/`. The group kill and the working-directory
census found every other member. On macOS, the process-group kill alone also left M3 alive, the
member that starts a new session. Killing the supervisor outright left every member alive on macOS,
as `R-STATE-9` and `R-STATE-10` expect.

I recommend a delegated cgroup ended by cgroup.kill for Linux under WSL2, and a Job Object with
kill-on-job-close for native Windows. Both are under "Recommendations", with what would reverse
each. This report hands over evidence. It changes no code, requirement or recorded decision.

| Run | Host | Ending | Route the supervisor was started by |
|---|---|---|---|
| m1 | laptop (macOS) | E1, process-group kill | `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/runners/mac-run.sh`, run locally on the laptop |
| m2 | laptop | E1c, today's route | the same |
| m3 | laptop | E2, `kill -9` of the supervisor | the same |
| w1, w7 | WSL2 | E1, cgroup.kill | `ssh hostpc wsl.exe -e bash -s` ran `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/runners/wsl-run.sh` inside WSL. That started the supervisor inside WSL as a transient systemd user service (`systemd-run --user`) |
| w2 | WSL2 | E1c, today's route | the same |
| w5 | WSL2 | E2, `kill -9` of the supervisor, then a fresh cgroup.kill writer | the same |
| w3 | WSL2 | E2, a first attempt | the same. Its fresh-writer loop ran on after the cgroup was removed (under "What failed"). w5 replaced it |
| w6 | WSL2 | E2 with systemd's default `KillMode` (supplementary) | the same |

| x3 | Windows | E2, `taskkill /F`, kill-on-job-close set | the same |
| x4 | Windows | E2, `taskkill /F`, kill-on-job-close not set, then a fresh process's reach | the same |

WSL was reached only through `ssh hostpc wsl.exe -e …`, because `wsl-hostpc` did not answer. In every
WSL run, the process that launched the tree, the Node supervisor, lived inside WSL. It was a child
of the user's systemd manager (pid 339), as each listing's parent pid column shows. It was never a
Windows process.

`docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/` holds the evidence:

- `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/transcript.txt`, the full transcript.
- `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/fixture/`, the fixture source for macOS and Linux.
- `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/fixture-win/`, the fixture source for Windows.

### E1, cgroup.kill (w1)

The supervisor wrote `1` to tree/cgroup.kill at 00:48:40.761Z. It logged `E1 cgroup.kill written;
cgroup.events populated 0 after 1.66 ms`, and w7 logged the same figure, 1.66 ms. That is a
measurement: two samples, each taken by the supervisor's 1 ms poll of cgroup.events. Twelve
seconds after the write:

```
listing run=w1 at 2026-11-20T00:48:52Z on Linux
PID     PPID    PGID    SID     STAT  CWD | CGROUP | ARGS
  reads `populated 0` or the cgroup is gone.
- Record the cgroup's path durably, so that the next start can write cgroup.kill to the orphan.
  That is the cgroup analogue of `R-STATE-10`, which w5's fresh writer showed works.

This closed every escape the fixture tried, including M6, which today's route left alive (w2). It
ended M5 without a `SIGTERM` stage, and it took 1.66 ms. It needed no root.

The next card must not assume the following:

## Round 2: every measurement repeated, with every process recorded

The round-1 judge found that round 1 recorded only the long-lived processes. It never recorded the
pids of short-lived helpers, or of the intermediate process that M4's parent is on macOS and Linux
("Every process the spike started", below). Those pids cannot be recovered, so round 2 repeated
every card measurement, on every host, on 2026-11-20, with every process the runs started
recorded. The stated intent for the new steps went up first, at
[1430#7044905230](https://github.com/williacj/rigger/issues/1430#issuecomment-7044905230).

### How each process was recorded

| Host | What recorded it |
|---|---|
| All | Every Node process loads `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/rerun/rec.cjs`. It writes the process's own pid and parent, and the pid, command and exit of every process it starts, as JSON lines. The laptop's step runner, `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/rerun/orch.cjs`, records each `ssh` and local command the same way, and writes `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/rerun/transcript-r2.txt`: each step's exact argv, its stdin script verbatim with sha256, its UTC start and end, and its full output |
| macOS and Linux | `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/rerun/fixture.sh` writes the pid of every process it starts, M4's parent included, using bash builtins only. The listing (`docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/rerun/list.cjs`) runs in Node, with one recorded `ps`. On macOS it adds one recorded `lsof -O` and one recorded `python3`, run by its real path rather than through the `/usr/bin/python3` stub |
| WSL | Each script fed to `bash -s` writes its own pid and its two WSL init parents, and runs every external command through a `rec` function that prints the command's pid, using bash builtins only. Each supervisor's unit prints the pid of its `id` and of its own bash, which then execs Node |
| Windows | A watcher, `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/rerun/watcher.ps1`, ran in its own SSH session from 16:34:25Z to 16:41:43Z. It logged every process start and stop on the machine through WMI's `Win32_ProcessStartTrace` and `Win32_ProcessStopTrace`. `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/rerun/collect-win.cjs` then picks out the spike's own processes: the watcher's session chain, every per-connection `sshd` that started within 3 s of one of the laptop's recorded `ssh` spawns, and every descendant of either. The result is `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/rerun/win-mine-full.json`. During the watch, sshd took 10 connections, and each one matched one of the spike's `ssh` spawns. Every pid that `rec.cjs` recorded on Windows is in that set |

Round 2 differs from round 1 only in how it records:

- The census and the macOS listing call `lsof -O`, so lsof does its kernel reads in its own
  process instead of forked helpers. The census reads the same fields as before.
- `fixture.sh` ends with `exec sleep 300` instead of `sleep 300`, so its tail makes no new process.
- The listing runs in Node instead of `list.sh`.
- WSL was reached only through `ssh hostpc wsl.exe -e`, as in round 1. The WSL machine booted again
  between sessions, because WSL stops when idle. The final check printed a boot time of 16:41:18Z,
  and its pids began again near 400.

### What round 2 measured

Every cell of the table below matches round 1. Times are in `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/rerun/transcript-r2.txt`.

## Round 2

    helpers and M4's parent are processes too.

    is left.

- **A full transcript is cheapest to produce from the session record.** Round 1's hand-kept

### 198. 2026-11-20T17:07:40.192Z -> 2026-11-20T17:07:40.580Z  host=laptop -> hostpc -> WSL  tool=Bash  {"description": "Fix remaining path spans in report"}
cd ~/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/1430-spike-engineer && python3 - <<'EOF'
import re
p='docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment.md'; s=open(p).read()
E='docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/rerun/'
full={'rec.cjs':E+'rec.cjs','fixture.sh':E+'fixture.sh','orch.cjs':E+'orch.cjs','list.sh':'docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/fixture/list.sh'}
bare={'cmd.exe','conhost.exe','node.exe','powershell.exe','taskkill.exe','whoami.exe','wsl.exe','wslhost.exe'}
def fix(m):
    x=m.group(1)
    if x in full: return '`'+full[x]+'`'
    if x in bare: return x
    return m.group(0)
s=re.sub(r'`([^`\n]+)`',fix,s); open(p,'w').write(s)
EOF

### The credential scan

Run at the pull request's head, over every file under `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/`:

```
$ grep -rnIiE 'gh[pousr]_[A-Za-z0-9]{20,}|github_pat_|sk-ant-|sk-[A-Za-z0-9]{20,}|AKIA[0-9A-Z]{16}|xox[baprs]-|BEGIN [A-Z ]*PRIVATE KEY|bearer [A-Za-z0-9._-]{20,}|(password|passwd|secret|token|api[_-]?key)[[:space:]]*[:=]' docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment; echo "grep exit $?"
grep exit 1
```

Exit 1 with no lines printed means no match in any of the 14 files. A second scan for email
addresses, `grep -rnIoE '[A-Za-z0-9._%+-]+@[A-Za-z0-9.-]+\.[a-z]{2,}'`, found only the systemd unit
name `user@1000.service`. The one account address `whoami /groups` printed was redacted before it
was committed.
